<meta>This page continues the author's preface, establishing the originality of his work and defining his specific approach to chronology. It follows a discussion of his motivations and precedes his detailed explanation of historical "proportions." Catchword: ranked.</meta>

### The Subject and Division of this Treatise.

I do not respect those who copy other men's writings and impudently claim them as their own. Just as there are beggars who steal the children of good families—and even maim them—to improve their own miserable condition, so there are many who have nothing of their own to write, yet publish the work of others and claim to be the authors. What is more common than this kind of <term>rapine</term>? <note>original: rapine; meaning the violent seizure of someone else's property or, in this context, intellectual theft.</note> But I hope it will be clear that I am far from doing this. If I happen to say anything that has already been written (which might occur without my knowledge), it is insignificant. Indeed, I have avoided including observations I have seen in other writers that only come close to the subject I am now treating. I avoid such help as much as possible and would be ashamed to ask for it.

What I am writing about at this time is not a mere <term>chronology</term>, <note>original: Chronology; the arrangement of events in order of occurrence.</note> as might be imagined at first glance. The world is full of chronologists, and their only effort is to calculate how many years or how much time has passed since the Creation, since the birth of Christ, or since some other famous event. This is the extent of their <term>arithmetic</term>. <note>original: Arithmetick.</note> But we must employ a different kind of calculation that requires more skill; without it, all chronology is merely raw material without form.

All the time since the beginning of the world has proceeded according to specific measures and proportions; herein lies one of the most remarkable secrets of history. This wonderful symmetry of ages and years, spread out

<summary>The author asserts the originality of his work, distancing himself from plagiarists, and explains that his treatise focuses on the "proportions" and mathematical symmetry of history rather than just counting years.</summary>
<keywords>chronology, plagiarism, arithmetic, historical symmetry, original research, proportions of time</keywords>

--- Page 14 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of divine proportions in history, specifically focusing on the significance of biblical numbers (numerology) and the hidden structure God has placed within the passage of time. It continues from the previous page's mention of time being "remarkably ranked."</meta>

...ranked with such order, and so skillfully enclosed by the hand of the <term>Ancient of Days</term> <note>original: Antient of days; a title for God from the Book of Daniel, representing His eternal nature.</note>, it produces reflections that make His incomparable wisdom appear.

And not only the times, but the generations and successions—of kingdoms and families, and even of individuals—are measured and proportioned. It should be observed that each generation, each family, and each person is marked with a specific number and a particular character. We know that numbers do not cause what happens; instead, they are marks or seals that God impresses upon His works so that we may distinguish them by their different codes.

The <term>Septenary</term> <note>original: Septenary; the number seven, often used in the Bible to signify completion or perfection.</note> is very frequent and common, being the number of the complete Creation. The numbers ten, twelve, forty, and others appear in turn for various meanings. Could it be said that the Holy Spirit was pleased to point out an empire that later caused so many troubles, marking the name of a man with three numerals that signify 666?

But some will say, "Scripture expresses these numbers only very seldom, and even less the proportions we speak of." It also does not state exactly how many kings the Kingdom of Judah has had before the <term>Captivity</term> <note>original: Captivity; the Babylonian Captivity, a period in biblical history when the Jewish people were forcibly relocated to Babylon.</note>, but only provides a catalog of them from the first to the last. Yet, Scripture speaks to those who know how to calculate. Moreover, God willed that the secrets of these measures, correspondences, oppositions, and distributions of times,

<summary>The author argues that history is not random but structured by God using specific numerical patterns and proportions, such as the number seven and the number of the beast (666). He suggests that while Scripture may not always explicitly state these totals, they are there for those who know how to calculate and study historical successions.</summary>
<keywords>numerology, biblical chronology, Ancient of Days, Septenary, 666, Kingdom of Judah, Babylonian Captivity, divine providence</keywords>

times, and what is contained within them, should be wrapped in covers, so that we might use our diligence to unfold them. And truly, although studying them may be difficult at first, we shall find very sweet flowers there, as well as the fruits of the Tree of Life.

But here is the main challenge—the primary opposition raised against me: that the <term>Chronology</term> <note>original: Chronology; the arrangement of events in order of occurrence, often used in a biblical context to calculate the age of the world and the timing of prophecy.</note> serving as the basis of my work is debatable in certain points, and therefore I base many debatable observations upon it. To which I answer:

1. That I have many good justifications for this Chronology, and that it existed long before me.
2. That I can defend and resolve the difficulties within it.
3. That when <term>Chronologers</term> <note>original: Chronologers; scholars who study historical records to determine the dates of past events.</note> disagree, we must always prefer the <term>calculation</term> <note>original: supputation; a formal calculation or estimation of dates or numbers.</note> where some mysterious point aligns with the circumstances of the time. 

What! Shall the variety of our opinions, which stem from our own imperfections, nullify the wonders of God?

Furthermore, the things I present cannot be denied without contradicting the Scripture; nor can the Harmonies I have found be dismissed without renouncing the skill of mathematics; nor can they be considered accidental without denying the providence of God; nor can it be said they have purely natural causes without blaspheming against His wisdom.

The beginning of time shall be the beginning of this work. --- Page 16 ---
<meta>This page continues the author's argument that chronological patterns are like a "divine alphabet." He outlines the structure of his book, which will trace history from the first Adam to the destruction of the Second Temple, and eventually through the New Testament.</meta>

...a very necessary alphabet for the most learned. All the highest matters that exist have need of an *A B C* to express them; just as the most eloquent language cannot be pronounced except through the sounds of alphabetical letters.

And since the Sun had the primary responsibility for marking and measuring out time, we will begin with him. It will appear that he himself was marked out and measured by time.

Afterwards, we shall see the general measurements of time passed from the creation of the first *Adam* to the Ascension of the second <note>original: "the Second"; referring to Jesus Christ, often called the "Last Adam" or "Second Adam" in Christian theology.</note>—indeed, even to the destruction of the second Temple. After that, we will look at various segments of time within the Old Testament and the events contained in them. Then we will pause until we reach the specific measurements of the New Testament, which shall form the second part of this treatise.

One day tells another, and one night informs another; how much more does one generation inform another, and one age inform another? How much more knowledge, then, results from the agreement and coordination of all the ages together, and of all the generations, which correspond so closely with one another? In the diversity of their tones, measures, and rhythms, we shall hear an excellent and most divine harmony.

### To the most distinguished gentleman in every respect,

## JOHN DESPAGNE, preacher of the Divine Word in the French Church of Westminster.

->*Regarding his Harmony of Times <note>original: "Harmoniam temporum," referring to a work of biblical chronology</note>, printed during the winter.*<-

### EPIGRAM <note>original: "EPIGRAMMA"</note>

Where do you find this passion, venerable old man,
> By which your creative vein <note>original: "vena," referring to the source of his poetic or intellectual inspiration</note> glows in the middle of winter?
From where do your writings, born under the star of the North Pole <note>original: "Arctoi sydere nata poli," implying the cold climate of London or the Northern Hemisphere</note>,
> Pour out such light and heat at the same time?
Is it that the seeds sown long ago by your native soil
> Have multiplied and swollen under such deep cold?
Is it that the smaller you are in stature, the greater is your spirit;
> And you yourself, though short, hide immense riches?
Or rather, inspired by divine love, do you show
> How great is the radiance of genius when combined with piety?

->*The First Part of the*<-
### *HARMONY of the TIMES, etc.*

---

### *The day on which the sun stood still, and served as a Sabbath day for Joshua, occurred after the sun was created in the year 2,555 (seven times three hundred and sixty-five). This is a number that corresponds to the three hundred and sixty-five days of the sun's annual cycle, just as the number seven corresponds to the Sabbath.*

<image-desc>A decorative woodcut initial 'T' featuring floral and vine motifs.</image-desc>

THE history in the tenth chapter of *Joshua* <note>The biblical book of Joshua, chapter 10, describes the battle of Gibeon where the sun and moon stood still</note> recounts the year of that miracle, but it is easy to find it. The *Israelites* who came out of the wilderness entered into the Land of *Canaan* in the year

<meta>catchword: year</meta>

<summary>Jean d'Espagne begins his chronological study by calculating the exact year of the miracle in Joshua 10, arguing that the timing (2,555 years after Creation) holds symbolic significance by combining the solar cycle with the Sabbatical number seven.</summary>
<keywords>Joshua, Israelites, Canaan, Sabbath, solar cycle, biblical chronology, Gibeon miracle, Harmony of the Times</keywords>

--- Page 26 ---
<meta>This page continues the chronological calculation from the previous page, detailing the events between the Israelites' entry into Canaan and the day the sun stood still.</meta>

<page-num>2</page-num>

[the] year of the Creation 2553. Between that time and the moment when the Sun stood still, many events intervened. These included their passage through the *Jordan* River, the seven days spent circling *Jericho*, the siege and sacking of *Ai*, the conspiracy of various kings of *Canaan*, the arming and mustering of several nations to oppose *Israel*, the matter of the *Gibeonites*, the five kings who assaulted them, the siege of *Gibeon*, the aid brought by *Joshua* to them, the battle fought there, and other details. 

So many achievements and exploits, which followed one after another before the Sun stood still, were the work of more than one year. If we calculate them to be two years—as all appearances suggest—we shall find that it was in the year of the Creation 2555 when the Sun stood still. Now, these 2,555 years are seven times 365, or 365 "weeks of years." <note>A "week of years" is a period of seven years, a common unit in biblical chronology based on the Sabbatical cycle.</note>

Even if we granted that this miracle occurred a little sooner—namely, in the year 2554—it was nevertheless toward the end of that last cycle of 365 weeks of years, which corresponds to the days in the Sun's annual course.

When we say 365 days, we implicitly include the additional five hours and minutes that exceed the 365 days; for antiquity

<summary>Jean d'Espagne lists the military campaigns of Joshua to argue that the miracle of the sun happened in the year 2555 after Creation. He highlights the mathematical harmony of this date, noting it represents 365 cycles of seven years.</summary>
<keywords>Joshua, Canaan, Gibeon, Jericho, biblical chronology, solar cycle, weeks of years, creation date, 2555 AM</keywords>

--- Page 27 ---
<meta>This page continues the author's argument that the Sun stood still in the year 2,555 after Creation, which marks a significant mathematical and symbolic milestone in biblical chronology.</meta>

<page-num>[3]</page-num>
<sig>B 2</sig>

[An]tiquity also had its intercalations <note>original: "intercalcations"; the insertion of extra days or months into a calendar to keep it aligned with the solar year</note> of days, and its embolisms <note>original: "emboliſms"; the insertion of an entire leap month into a lunar calendar</note> or insertions of months. The Scripture presupposes these when it mentions specific numbers of years. However, we only express the count as 365 days to avoid the frequent repetition of the extra hours and minutes. Thus, we also understand that every one of these years contains 365 days, 5 hours, and about 49 minutes.

But as for that great wonder which is now under our consideration, the timing of when it occurred makes it even more illustrious. Truly, it is not surprising that the **Sun**, being about to finish the 365 weeks of years <note>A "week of years" is a seven-year cycle. 365 such cycles equals 2,555 years.</note> of its course, was granted a day of **Rest**. Man was commanded to rest himself on the seventh day; the ground rests on the seventh year; and the **Sun**—about to finish seven times as many years as it takes days to complete a single year—received a command to rest for the space of one day.

This measurement, which contains 365 years, may be called a "year of years." Just as 365 days make up an ordinary year (which is a year of days), so 365 years make up a "year of years." We give it this name even though it is not found in the text of the Scriptures or in standard chronology. Words are interchangeable,

<summary>Jean d'Espagne argues that the miracle of the sun standing still occurred exactly 2,555 years after Creation, representing a symbolic "Sabbath" for the sun after it completed 365 cycles of seven years.</summary>
<keywords>chronology, solar cycle, intercalation, embolism, Sabbath, Joshua, year of years, 2555 AM</keywords>

--- Page 28 ---
<meta>This page continues the author's argument that the timing of the Sun standing still was not coincidental, but was mathematically and symbolically significant in the context of the history of Creation.</meta>

<page-num>4</page-num>

rent, provided they are intelligible. And thus the Sun, being about to finish its seventh "year of years," stood still to rest itself.

Certainly, God in His wisdom was pleased that this miracle should occur at such a time rather than at any other. His intent in commanding the Sun to halt was chiefly so that the *Israelites* might have light, by which they might be better enabled to pursue their enemies. But this reason, although mentioned on its own, does not exclude the other reasons that accompany it. On many other occasions where the *Israelites* had need of the Sun standing still, God was not pleased to work this wonder at all. To demonstrate that God had a further aim here than only completing the victory of *Joshua*, we ought to consider what is easy to observe: that all the earth felt this marvel <note>original: "prodigy"</note>. For while one part of the world beheld this long day, the other part experienced a night just as long. What, then, was the cause of this strange interruption of the Sun’s course? Since the greatest part of the world worshiped that flaming body as a god <note>original: "Deity"</note>, the ceasing of its motion served to declare that there was a Superior Power who could stop its course, and inhibit or suspend its operations.

This miracle, moreover, occurred most

<summary>The author explains that while the Sun stood still to help Joshua in battle, the miracle also served a global purpose: to prove to sun-worshiping nations that a higher Power controls the heavens.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the explanation of why God chose a specific moment in history for the Sun to stand still, linking it to the Israelites' arrival in Canaan and the symbolic importance of the number seven.</meta>

<page-num>[5]</page-num>
<sig>B 3</sig>

most appropriately at that time; the Israelites had newly entered into the Land of Canaan, which was full of idolaters and surrounded by nations that adored the Sun, the Moon, and all the host of heaven <note>original: "Hoſt of Heaven"—the stars and celestial bodies, which ancient neighboring cultures often worshiped as deities</note>. God, by this miracle, warned <note>original: "advertis'd"</note> his people that they should not allow themselves to be drawn away to such abominations. <margin>*Deuteronomy 17:2, 3*</margin> And to refute <note>original: "confute"</note> those nations by a visible and <term>irrefragable</term> (undeniable) argument, He caused both the Sun and the Moon to stand still at the word of a man.

But there is yet a more specific reason why this miracle occurred at the time we have mentioned. The <term>septenary number</term> (the number seven) of times, days, years, and ages—and of their periods and revolutions—is a characteristic sign by which we know God has marked most of His works. It is remembered how God rested on the seventh day, having finished the Creation. For this cause, He intended that the Sun, which is the measure of days and years, should rest for one day in its seventh "year of years." This happened exactly when the Sun had accomplished its 365 "weeks of years" (or seven times 365 years), which is seven times as many years as the Sun had run days in a single year. Finally, just as every seventh year was a year of Sabbath, the Sun observed a Sabbath in the seventh year of its 365 weeks of years, which was itself a year of Sabbath.

<summary>The author explains that the Sun's halt served two purposes: to prove God's power over the celestial bodies worshiped by the Canaanites and to mark a "Sabbath" for the Sun itself after a grand cycle of 2,555 years.</summary>

<page-num>6</page-num>

How much light meets in this wonderful Sabbath of the Sun! That luminary <note>original: "Luminary"—a celestial body that gives off light, in this case, the sun</note>, which gives light to all the rest of the world, was itself enlightened on that day. *"He knows the time of his going down"* (*Psalm* 104:19), but the sun did not know until then that there would be a day when his setting would be later than usual. The sun can never give as much light to our eyes as these considerations bestow on our spirits.

Behold then a remarkable coincidence <note>original: "admirable concurrence"</note>: 365 days in a year, and 365 years corresponding to them; 365 weeks of years (or seven times 365 years) had expired when the sun stood still. At that moment, the sun's numerical pattern <note>original: "cipher"</note> met with that of the Sabbath, as the total of 365 was measured out by cycles of seven.

But let us behold another miracle which He has shown us in heaven.

### *In the Tenth Year of the Tenth Year of Years, expired since the Creation, the Sun went back Ten Degrees, Isaiah 38:8.*

Behold yet a wonder that parallels or surpasses the former; it occurred when the sun was 3,295 years old.

Let us calculate as we did above: a period of 365 years is a "year of years" <note>The author defines a "year of years" as a cycle of 365 years, mirroring the 365 days in a single year</note>, and 3,285 years is exactly nine times 365. Ten years after that—which was the year 3,295 <note>The original OCR reads 3285, but the author's mathematical argument (9 x 365 + 10) and the previous page's context indicate the year 3,295</note>—the backward movement of that great celestial body <note>original: "Luminary"—referring to the sun</note> occurred. These ten years were the first ten of the tenth "year of years" to pass since the Creation. 

So it follows that when the sun went back, it had reached the tenth year of its tenth "year of years"; the ten degrees of its backward movement <term>retrogradation</term> seemed clearly to signify this. In short, in the tenth year of the tenth cycle of 365 years, the sun went back by ten degrees. What person is so dull-witted that they are not filled with wonder <note>original: "ravished in admiration"</note> at such heavenly harmony?

Add to this that in this interval of ten years, which ended with the backward movement of the sun, the forced migration <note>original: "transmigration"</note> of the Ten Tribes of *Israel* occurred, when they were carried away into *Assyria*.

### *The History of the Old Testament consists of ten Prophetical Years; and from its end until the death of Christ, there is a space of another Prophetical Year.*

In the language of the Prophets, one day is often used to represent a year, and a "Prophetical Year" consists of 360 solar <note>original: "solary"</note> years. For example, in Revelation <note>original: "Revel."</note> 12:6 and 12:14, "one thousand two hundred and sixty days" are equated to "a time, and times, and half a time." This refers to three and a half Prophetical Years, where three hundred and sixty solar years are counted as one Prophetical Year.

Now, as to why God reckons 360 ordinary years as only one Prophetical Year, we might find an explanation <note>original: "conjecture"</note> in the analogy of a natural celestial cycle <note>original: "revolution"</note> that occurred within such a timeframe. For at the end of 360 years—and not before—the measurement of the lunar <note>original: "Lunary"</note> year, according to astronomers, aligns with that of the solar year. But there is a more direct and obvious reason: among the Hebrews, the year they called the "Civil Year" consisted of only 360 days. As for the five extra <term>supernumerary</term> days,

<summary>The author defines a "Prophetical Year" as 360 solar years, citing the Book of Revelation and the alignment of lunar and solar cycles as evidence for this unit of time.</summary>

<keywords>Prophetical Year, biblical chronology, Book of Revelation, solar year, lunar year, Hebrew Civil Year, numerology, astronomy</keywords>

...days, they were found every sixth year, at which point a month was added, called **Embolismical** <term>Embolismical: Also known as an intercalary month, this is an extra month inserted into a calendar to keep it synchronized with the seasons or solar year</term>. As for the extra hours beyond the 365 days of the year, there was yet another **Embolism** <term>Embolism: The insertion of leap days or months</term>, or addition, to account for them every one hundred and twenty years; alternatively, from time to time, they inserted one or more days. 

Just as the ordinary year then consisted of 360 days, the **Prophetical year** <term>Prophetical year: A period of 360 solar years, used by biblical chronologists to interpret prophetic timelines</term> consisted of 360 <note>original: "365"; however, the author's calculation of 3,600 years for ten cycles confirms 360 is intended</note> natural years. Therefore, from the Creation to the year 3600, there are ten Prophetical years—that is, ten periods of 360 solar <note>original: "Solary"</note> years. 

The history of the Old Testament ends exactly with the tenth Prophetical year. We determine this according to the chronological order of events—which is our current focus—rather than the order in which the books are arranged in the Bible. This prince reigned for only six years and was defeated by Alexander the Great in the year of the world 3600. This was the final year of the tenth Prophetical year, completing ten cycles of 360 years. It is at this point that the history of the Old Testament concludes.

### *The history of the Old Testament spans ten Prophetical Years; and from its conclusion until the death of Christ, there is a span of another Prophetical Year.*

In the language of the biblical prophets, one "day" is often used to represent a year, and a **Prophetical year** <term>Prophetical year: A period of 360 solar years, used by biblical chronologists to interpret prophetic timelines</term> consists of 360 solar <note>original: "solary"</note> years. For example, in *Revelation* 12:6 and 12:14, *one thousand two hundred and sixty days* are described as *a time, and times, and half a time.* This refers to three and a half prophetical years, with three hundred and sixty solar years being counted as one prophetical year.

Now, as to why God counts 360 ordinary years as only one "Prophetical year," we might find a clue in the pattern of natural cycles that occur within such a period. According to astronomers, it is only at the end of 360 years that the measurement of the lunar year aligns with that of the solar <note>original: "Sun"</note> year. However, there is a more immediate and obvious reason for this: among the Hebrews, the year they called the "Civil Year" consisted of only 360 days. As for the five extra <note>original: "supernumerary"</note> days,

There were twenty-two generations in the lineage of our Savior from the Babylonian Captivity to Christ the Prince.

## The twenty-two generations from the Captivity, inclusive, until Christ.

These generations are found in the genealogy recorded in the third chapter of *St. Luke*. He presents the lineage from one perspective, just as *St. Matthew* had represented it from another; this explains the difference in calculations <note>original: "supputations"</note> between these two Evangelists. *St. Luke* traces the lineage backward, and these are the number of the generations <note>original: "degrees"</note>.

It is true that the history of the Old Testament (*1 Chronicles 3:19*) names Pedaiah <note>original: "Pedaia"</note> as the father of Zerubbabel <note>original: "Zorobabel"</note> instead of Shealtiel <note>original: "Salathiel"</note>, who was his uncle. Nevertheless, it is clear that in counting upward from Christ to the Babylonian Captivity—during which the father of Zerubbabel lived—there are precisely twenty-two generations.

To further summarize all the parts of this divine plan <note>original: "Divine Oeconomy"; this refers to the way God manages and orders human history.</note>, we can observe the following:

Before the Church became a nation: **22** Patriarchs.

From the establishment of the priesthood until the destruction of the first Temple: **22** generations in the High Priesthood.

From Israel's entrance into Canaan until the first king: **22** judges or governors.

From the beginning of the monarchy <note>original: "Kingship"</note> until its end <note>original: "suppression of it"</note>: **22** kings.

From the Babylonian Captivity until Christ: **22** generations.

This is also very remarkable: during the twenty-two earliest generations of the world, God renewed the promise concerning the "seed of the woman" <note>A reference to the prophecy of the Messiah in Genesis 3:15.</note>. For Jacob, who was the twenty-second patriarch, spoke of Shiloh <note>A messianic title from Genesis 49:10 often interpreted as the Prince of Peace or the Messiah.</note>, the "seed of the woman" who was to come. And the twenty-second generation after the ancestors of Shiloh were transported to Babylon, where

Most of these septenaries begin with some illustrious sign <note>original: "mark"</note> of the person of Christ, who was to conclude these genealogies.

The first begins with God himself, to show the generation <note>The lineage or origin.</note> of his Son.

The second begins with *Enoch* <note>original: "Enoc"</note>, the seventh man from *Adam*. He was the third person to leave the world <note>The author likely counts Abel and Adam as the first two to depart, though Enoch's departure was unique.</note>, and the first who ascended bodily into heaven. The third begins with *Eber* <note>original: "Heber"</note>. During his lifetime, the confusion of tongues occurred at *Babel*. Christ, after his ascension into heaven, performed a completely opposite miracle by bestowing the gift of tongues <note>A reference to the miracle of Pentecost described in the Book of Acts.</note>.

But why did he not express it more briefly as a total sum, instead of using such segments and broken numbers? There are many significant reasons for this, which we will address in the appropriate place. Likewise, why is it enough to speak of the total number of years that have passed since the Creation without dividing them into unequal parts? Simply looking at the total does not show the proportions and stages from one to the other; yet from their inequality, a certain harmony <note>original: "melody"</note> results.

It is a phrase that seems to have been proverbial since the beginning of the world to exaggerate or amplify a matter by placing one seven followed by seventy times seven: *“Cain shall be avenged sevenfold, but Lamech seventy-sevenfold”* (Genesis 4:24). And our Lord commands us to forgive *“not only seven times, but seventy times seven”* (Matthew 18:22). A similar pattern of multiplication may be observed in most of God's works, especially in the divine management <note>original: "œconomy" (referring to God's administration or stewardship of history)</note> of Time, for the reasons mentioned above. [Na]tivity of Christ in the year of the world 3928, a little after the beginning of that year. If we subtract the months of Christ’s time in his mother’s womb, it follows that he was conceived in the year 3927. At that point, the Virgin conceived according to the Promise; then *the Word was made flesh* <note>John 1:14</note>.

Now, this number of 3927 is entirely composed of septenaries <note>original: "Septenaries"; groups of seven</note>. The first is a single seven; the second is multiplied by the first; and the third is multiplied by the second. As for the reasoning behind the connections, divisions, order, and arrangement of the septenaries that make up those times, I could offer some conjectures; however, it must be enough for me to marvel <note>original: "admire"</note> that those stages <note>original: "gradations"</note>, so variously proportioned, reached their end when God sent his Son, born of a woman—a moment which the Apostle calls *the fullness of time* (Galatians 4:4).

### From the Fall of the first Adam to the Ascension of the Second <note>The "Second Adam" refers to Jesus Christ</note> (which was in the 33rd year of Christ's residence on Earth), there passed thirty-three times one hundred years <note>3,300 years</note>.

It was the number of the years of *Moses*; it is the third part of a Prophetical year, namely <note>original: "viz."</note> 360 years; it was the fourth part of the time which passed from the departure out of *Egypt* to the foundation of the Temple of *Solomon*, namely 480 years; it was the eighth part of the time which passed from the completion of the first Temple until the time of the tearing of the veil of the Temple at the death of Christ, namely 960 years. <meta>This page continues the author's numerical calculation, concluding that the entire span of history from Creation to the Ascension of Christ consists of thirty-three cycles of 120 years, mirroring the thirty-three years of Christ’s life.</meta>

twenty years. For from the Creation to the thirty-third and last year of Christ’s residence <note>original: "abode"</note> on earth, we calculate 3,960 years. This number is exactly thirty-three times 120 <note>33 x 120 = 3,960</note>. That entire span of time, therefore, contains as many segments as there were years in the life of Jesus Christ in this world. For the timeframe of our Redemption, God has multiplied 120 years thirty-three times; this period concluded in the thirty-third year of Christ, which was the year of his death and his Ascension.

### The different periods of time that God in his patience grants to sinners before he punishes them.

### A Consideration of the Age of Noah when he was Commanded to Build the Ark

He had already lived 480 years, which is four times 120 years; to this number, God added 120 more years, which concluded at the Great Flood <note>original: "Deluge"</note>. This timeframe <note>original: "term"</note> which God set for the world maintained a just and exact proportion corresponding to the years of that Patriarch <note>Noah</note>. A man’s years are but portions <note>original: "parcels"</note> of time within the total duration of the world; yet, the 120 years the world lasted before it perished by the Flood were themselves but a portion of a single man’s total lifespan.

### The years of Noah after the Flood were double the years of Abraham.

### Ten times forty years expired at the departure from Egypt. The tenth and last forty years ended when Moses was twice forty years old. Another forty years in the Wilderness ended with the last forty years of Moses' life.

### *Two Miracles with opposite effects: one in the seventh generation after Adam, and the other in the fourteenth generation after the seventh.*

In the seventh generation after *Adam*, *Enoch* was translated <note>original: "Enoc was translated"; meaning he was taken to heaven by God without dying</note>. And in the fourteenth generation after *Enoch*, the wife of *Lot* was turned into a statue <note>of salt</note>. These were two opposite effects on human bodies: one was exalted to heaven, and the other was fastened and riveted to the earth.

Who would have thought that a dream of thirteen luminaries <note>original: "Luminaries"; celestial bodies, specifically the sun, moon, and eleven stars</note>, who were to do homage to him, would have included a servitude of thirteen years? *Joseph* was seventeen years old when he had this dream, which promised him dominion <note>original: "Domination"</note> over all his brothers. Shortly afterward, he was sold and remained a slave until he was thirty years old, at which time he interpreted *Pharaoh's* dreams. Here are thirteen years during which he was a slave, following the dream that had promised him authority <note>original: "Superiority"</note> over the thirteen highest-ranking members of his family.

A situation often lies hidden within its opposite. The thirteen luminaries seemed to predict <note>original: "prognosticate"</note> nothing but prosperity for *Joseph*; however, before he came to have authority over those thirteen luminaries, he had to be a slave for as many years as he had seen luminaries doing homage to him.

### The Dead doing homage to the Living.

In *Joseph's* dream, the moon that did homage to him signified his mother, as *Jacob* interpreted it in Genesis 37. This could not have been *Rachel*, for she was already dead.

<summary>This page explores the symbolic irony in the life of Joseph, noting that his thirteen years of slavery corresponded to the thirteen luminaries in his dream, and addresses the interpretation of the moon representing his mother.</summary>

<keywords>Joseph, Pharaoh, Jacob, Rachel, Genesis 37, Dream of thirteen Luminaries, servitude, biblical interpretation, numerology</keywords>

--- Page 86 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of Joseph’s dream in Genesis 37, specifically addressing the logical difficulty of how his mother could bow to him if she were already deceased at the time of the prophecy's fulfillment.</meta>

...before this dream happened; nor was it Leah, for she died before Jacob went to Joseph in Egypt (Genesis 49:31). How then, being dead, did she pay homage <note>original: "Homage"; a formal public acknowledgment of superiority or honor</note> to Joseph? She did so through the persons of her children. Leah, who held the place of a mother to Joseph when this dream happened, and who died before its fulfillment, nevertheless served to fulfill it. Although she was dead, she bowed before Joseph; she spoke; she begged <note>original: "prayed"; here meaning an earnest request for help rather than a religious prayer</note> him to give her food. The acts of submission and the requests that her children made to Joseph were effectively made by Leah herself, as she was represented by her children. Similarly, Rachel, having been dead for many ages, bewailed <note>original: "did bewail"; lamented or cried out in grief</note> her children—even those who were born 1,660 years after her death (Matthew 2).

### Of the twelve sons of Jacob, only two were without blemish.
### Of the twelve spies who went to Canaan, only two were faithful to God.
### Of the twelve tribes, only two remained faithful to God and to the House of David.

All the sons of *Jacob*, with the exception of *Joseph* and *Benjamin*, were guilty of serious sins. *Reuben* and *Judah* were incestuous <note>Reuben committed adultery with his father’s concubine (Genesis 35:22), and Judah with his daughter-in-law Tamar (Genesis 38)</note>, and the eight others plotted the death of *Joseph* and ultimately sold him into slavery. All the spies sent from the wilderness to the land of *Canaan*, except for *Caleb* and *Joshua*, returned with a bad report of the land; they caused the people to complain <note>original: "murmur"</note> and provoked God's anger. Similarly, all the tribes, except for two <note>The tribes of Judah and Benjamin, which formed the Southern Kingdom</note>, abandoned the Temple and the true religion during the reign of *Jeroboam*.

<summary>The author draws a symbolic parallel between three groups of twelve in the Bible—Jacob's sons, the spies in Canaan, and the tribes of Israel—noting that in each case, only two remained righteous or faithful.</summary>

Some are of the opinion that all the Christians in the world—all who profess the Name of Christ—are barely a sixth part of mankind, or two out of twelve. But who knows how many further sub-divisions can be made? Of ten lepers who were healed <note>original: "made clean"; see Luke 17:11–19</note>, where are the other nine? Only one out of ten possessed a spirit of gratitude.

### *Regarding the ten children of Job and the ten righteous people who might have prevented the destruction of Sodom: A significant question based on a comparison of these two examples.*

For the sake of ten righteous people (had they been found in *Sodom*), God would have spared that city, which was so abominable. However, the ten children of *Job* were not spared; instead, they perished under the ruins of a single house. If they were righteous—since the historical account does not accuse <note>original: "tax"</note> them of any great wickedness <note>original: "enormity"</note>—it is strange that God, having offered to spare an entire city of abominable sinners for the sake of ten righteous people, would allow ten righteous people to perish in a single family. If we say

say that the children of *Job* were sinners, as *Bildad* seems to imply in *Job* 8:4, we know he said as much about *Job* himself, though falsely. But suppose *Job's* children were wicked: could not ten wicked ones be spared for the life of one righteous man (especially since he was their father), just as a thousand, or perhaps ten thousand, wicked people in *Sodom* could have been spared for the sake of ten righteous? Toward the house of *Job*, where the head of the family was righteous, God did not maintain as favorable a proportion as he would have for *Sodom*, had that city possessed as many righteous people as *Job* had children.

The reasons for this difference are beyond our understanding <note>original: "above us"</note>; nevertheless, we might say that we should not find it strange if the discipline <note>original: "corrections"; referring to divine trials or fatherly chastisement</note> of those whom death carries <note>original: "transmit"</note> to heaven is sometimes harsher than the earthly <note>original: "temporal"</note> punishments of those who, despite everything, go afterward to everlasting fire, such as the people of *Sodom* (*Jude* 1:7).

### Job and Jeremiah <note>original: "Jeremy"</note> have cursed the day of their birth <note>original: "Nativity"</note>, but have not mentioned which year or month it was.

Since they spoke of erasing the memory of that day and removing it from the calendar, we should not be surprised that they withhold the date.

However, I have noted elsewhere (in my commentary on the Creed) that Scripture never says on what day a person is born; it never records anyone's birthday. It does not even state the day on which *Jesus Christ* was born. What I have already observed on that matter does not need to be repeated here.

### Job's sons are not named in Scripture, yet his daughters are named there.

<margin>Job 42:13, 14, 15.</margin>
Each of the three daughters of *Job* has a name and, in addition, a glorious title <term>epithet: a descriptive term or title</term>,

<summary>The author observes that the Bible intentionally omits birth dates, including those of Job, Jeremiah, and Jesus, and notes the rare distinction given to Job's daughters, who are named while his sons are not.</summary>
<keywords>Job, Jeremiah, birthdays, biblical chronology, Jesus Christ, Job's daughters, naming conventions, genealogy</keywords>

--- Page 93 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion from the previous page regarding the naming of Job's children in the Bible, specifically comparing the honor given to his daughters versus his sons.</meta>

Their beauty is also mentioned; but as for the seven sons he had, not one of them is named. This goes against the usual custom of Scripture, which in its genealogies <term>genealogies: lists of family ancestors and descendants</term> typically records the names of males, except when a family line <term>race: family lineage or biological line</term> fails. Should we suggest that *Job* had forgotten, or thought it unimportant, to give names to his children? Or were they less worthy than their sisters? It is a great honor for a godly <note>original: "pious"</note> man to have his name recorded in the holy history; yet the names of all the sons of *Job* are buried in the same grave with them, without an epitaph <note>original: "Epitaph"; an inscription on a tombstone</note>, while his daughters are mentioned there with honor. 

If we knew the successes, whether good or bad, of *Job’s* descendants <term>posterity: descendants or future generations</term>, we might understand the reasons for this distinction. However, since the primary aim of Scripture is the genealogy of the *Son of God*, it shortens the family records of everyone from whom *Christ* did not descend biologically <note>original: "according to the flesh"</note>, even if they were distinguished <note>original: "illustrious"</note> in other ways.

<summary>The author explores why Job's sons are left unnamed in the Bible while his daughters are honored by name, concluding that Scripture prioritizes the direct lineage of Jesus Christ over other family histories.</summary>
<keywords>Job, genealogy, naming conventions, biblical history, posterity, lineage of Christ, daughters of Job, biblical customs</keywords>

### From the division of tongues at Babel until the death of Heber, who was the father of the Hebrews, was 430 years.

### From the promise made to Abraham until the Law, was 430 years. From the Law until David was anointed in Bethlehem was a full 430 years.

The first of these parallels is clearly shown in *Genesis 10:25* and *11:17*. The division of languages <note>original: "Tongues"</note> <term>division of tongues: the biblical event at the Tower of Babel where the single human language was split into many</term> happened at the birth <note>original: "nativity"</note> of Peleg <note>original: "Phaleg"</note>, the son of Heber. After this, Heber lived another 430 years and even outlived Abraham. 

This patriarch Heber had seen the time when the holy language was the only one on earth, and he witnessed the beginning of the diversity of languages. However, he and his descendants <term>posterity: future generations or descendants</term> kept the language of their ancestors, and they were called "Hebrews" after Heber. It was fitting <note>original: "expedient"</note> that he, who was older than all the new languages of Babel, should continue to live for a long time after their origin.

<summary>The author outlines three 430-year periods in biblical history and explains the origin of the Hebrew language and name through the patriarch Heber.</summary>

<keywords>Heber, Peleg, Abraham, King David, Babel, Hebrew language, biblical chronology, Genesis, division of tongues</keywords>

--- Page 95 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of biblical chronology, specifically focusing on the repeated 430-year intervals between major redemptive events: the life of Heber, the Promise to Abraham, the giving of the Law, and the anointing of King David.</meta>

...so that the original language <note>original: "Primitive Tongue"</note> could be passed down through the families of all the children of God. Therefore, that period lasted 430 years.

A similar number of years passed from the Promise made to Abraham until the giving of the Law, immediately after the Exodus from the land of Egypt (*Galatians 3:17*).

Again, a similar span of 430 years occurred from the giving of the Law (in the year 2513 <note>This date follows a traditional Anno Mundi or "Year of the World" chronology starting from Creation</note>) until the anointing of David by Samuel in Bethlehem, at the end of the year 2943 or the start of the following year.

The Promise, the Law, and the Kingship of David—the ancestor of Christ—were each distributed across equal intervals of time.

### *The waters of Egypt remained blood for seven days. The Sabbath day was forbidden to the Egyptians. A hidden power <note>original: "secret virtue"</note> of the number seven <term>Septinary number: relating to the number seven; a "septenary" group</term> regarding the waters is acknowledged.*

<meta>This page continues the discussion on the significance of the number seven and the divine timing of the plagues of Egypt, specifically focusing on the first plague where water was turned to blood.</meta>

### ...acknowledged by the Naturalists.

The waters of *Egypt*, turned into blood—or rather, the blood which had previously been water—remained in that place for seven whole days. One of those seven was supposed to be a day of Sabbath, according to the ancient Ordinance of God. But He was not pleased to allow the *Egyptians* even one day of rest during that entire week. 

Because of the crisis caused by that miraculous <note>original: "prodigious"</note> blood, which had taken the place of the water, they were forced to dig into the ground to find something to drink. That labor continued for seven whole days, so that there was no Sabbath for them (*Exodus* 7:24–25). 

Furthermore, the Sabbath was intended to be a day of rejoicing, but that entire week was sorrowful for the *Egyptians*. They had taken from the *Hebrews* the freedom of the Sabbath, compelling them to work without any break or distinction of days; for one week, God repaid them in the same currency <note>original: "coyn"</note>. If they had been men of sound judgment, they might have recognized that God would not permit them to observe the Sabbath day because they had caused it to be violated by others.

But as for the seven days of that corruption of the waters: although it was supernatural,

<summary>This page discusses the first plague of Egypt, explaining that the water remained blood for seven days to deny the Egyptians a Sabbath rest as punishment for their oppression of the Hebrews.</summary>

<keywords>Plagues of Egypt, Sabbath, Exodus 7, divine justice, Hebrews, Egyptians, water into blood, number seven</keywords>

--- Page 97 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion on the symbolism of the number seven and how natural observations reflect divine laws, transitioning into a calculation of "Prophetical Years" in biblical history.</meta>

natural, it also brings to our memory the natural cause of an effect which the naturalists <note>original: "Naturalists"; referring to early scientists or natural philosophers</note> observe. They say that water which has been corrupted seven times, and purified seven times, never decays <note>original: "putrefie"</note> again. Wonderful is that cycle <note>original: "revolution"</note> which, having reached the seventh time, rests forever after, as if it had arrived at its Sabbath. It is not that the number seven itself possesses this power <note>original: "vertue"</note>, but rather that God has so ordained it; He decreed that such a cycle should cease when it reached that number. And even this serves as a memorial of the seventh day, on which all the works of God were completed.

Therefore, just as the sixth Prophetic year expired in the 77th year since the Promise, the seventh Prophetic year ended in the 7th year after the coming out from Egypt.

## The distinction of the Tribes has sometimes served to discipline the Israelites.

God, who was the founder of their state and the author of their civil order <note>original: "Policy"</note>, was pleased that the tribes should be distinct, most notably the royal tribe of Judah and the priestly <note>original: "Sacerdotal"</note> tribe of Levi. That distinction was necessary for many important reasons, yet it has often been the cause of rivalry <note>original: "emulation"</note> and division. The Ephraimites, thinking themselves slighted by Jephthah, engaged in battle with him; the Benjamites, to support <note>original: "countenance"</note> some wicked <note>original: "lewd"</note> people of their tribe, went to war with all the other Israelites. We know of the war between Judah and the other tribes for the kingship after the death of Saul, and also the dispute between them regarding the bringing back of David after the defeat of Absalom. Finally, the ten tribes abandoned the tribe of Judah and created a separate state and religion.

When the Israelites sinned against God, the distinction between the tribes often turned into division, and division into ruin. The same happened when they attempted to confuse the rights and

### *An Analogy of the seventh day, when no Manna fell, and of the seventh day of a person's hunger.*

On the Eve of the Sabbath, God had ordained that *Manna* should be gathered for the next day because none fell on the seventh day. The obvious <note>original: "manifest"</note> reason for that pause <note>original: "intermission"</note> was the holiness of the day of Rest, which was incompatible <note>original: "inconsistent"</note> with the manual labor required for gathering *Manna*. However, it seems there is yet another hidden meaning <note>original: "secret"</note> in that stopping of work on the seventh day.

It is certain—and there are many examples in the Scripture—that several divine mysteries are enclosed within certain natural secrets that reflect them. Now, it is the common opinion that a person cannot naturally endure hunger for more than seven days—that is <note>original: "viz." abbreviation for the Latin "videlicet"</note>, until there is no more chyle <term>chylous: relating to chyle, a milky fluid of sugar and fat formed in the small intestine during digestion</term> or nourishing fluid in the stomach or intestines. If a person goes beyond seven days without food, he dies. And because *Manna* did

### *A Mystery represented by the character of the day on which wounds are most painful.*

The third day has produced the fulfillment of many mysteries. On the third day after warning was given, God published the Law at *Sinai*. On this occasion, we turn to the consideration of another subject, expressed in *Hosea 6:2: On the third day he will raise us up.* These words carry an obvious <note>original: "manifest"</note> allusion to a significant circumstance regarding wounds. It is known that on the third day, they are more painful than on the first or second days, and the Scripture observes this in the history of the *Shechemites* <note>The people of Shechem, who were attacked by Simeon and Levi while recovering from circumcision.</note> in *Genesis 34:25*. The Prophet’s metaphor comes from that fact. The preceding words are clear <note>original: "express"</note> about this: *He has torn, but he will heal us; he has struck us,* <note>original: "smitten"</note>

### An instruction drawn from Leviticus 25: that the price of land was lower as the Year of Jubilee approached.

In the Year of Jubilee <note>In ancient Israel, a holy year occurring every 50 years when debts were forgiven and sold land was returned to its original family owners.</note>, all the lands that had been alienated <term>alienated: sold or transferred to another owner</term> returned to their former owners. Therefore, the sale price—which was essentially just a lease—was determined by the number of years remaining until the next Jubilee. ### A Lesson Drawn from a Disproportion Found in Leviticus 26:8

The words are: *Five of you shall chase a hundred, and a hundred of you shall put ten thousand to flight.* The proportion of five to a hundred is very different from the proportion of a hundred to ten thousand; the first is a ratio of one to twenty, but the other is one to a hundred.

### Leviticus 27: Certain Inequalities in the Valuation <note>original: "Estimation"</note> of Sex and Age for Those Being Ransomed.

They always paid more for a male than for a female, but not always in the same proportion. The tax <note>The "tax" refers to the "valuation" or "estimation" set by the law for dedicating a person to the Lord.</note> differed according to the difference of age, but with much variety.

From a month after birth <note>original: "nativity"</note> to the age of five years, the female paid three-fifths of the sum paid for a male.

From five years to twenty, the female paid exactly half the valuation of a man.

From twenty years to sixty, the female paid three-fifths again.

From sixty years to the end of her life, she paid two-thirds of the sum.

These inequalities of proportions have their reasons, although they are very hard to find. ### Three men who knew exactly <note>original: "punctually"</note>, and through divine revelation <note>original: "from above"</note>, the place where they should die.

Aaron, Moses, and Jesus Christ had that foreknowledge; no one else ever had it. A person may guess at the place of their death <note>original: "fatal place"</note>, but cannot be sure of it, even if they were already standing upon the scaffold. How many accidents have occurred between the neck and the sword? Only these three—the first priest in *Israel*, the first leader of that people, and the firstborn among the dead <note>A biblical title for Jesus, referring to his resurrection.</note>—have been certain of the place where they would die. Yet there was this difference: Aaron and Moses did not know the place of their death until a few hours or a few days before, but Jesus Christ knew the place of his death long in advance.

*Simeon* <note>A devout man in Jerusalem who was promised by the Holy Spirit that he would not die until he had seen the Messiah (Luke 2:25-32).</note> was forewarned that he should not die before he had seen the Christ.

Our Savior foretold that there were some who would not "taste of death" <note>An archaic idiom meaning to experience the act of dying.</note> until they had seen the Son of Man coming in His Kingdom (Matthew 16:28), but He named no one in particular.

Here is one difference between Christ and all those of whom we have spoken: He alone knew and foresaw not only the years, but the day, the very hour, and indeed the very moment of His death.

*God gives to one man as many days as He takes away from another—indeed, from a whole generation. The ordinary lifespan <note>original: "ages"</note> of the Israelites, who were condemned to die within the forty years of their wandering in the wilderness, was seventy years—the age of Joshua* <note>While Joshua lived to 110, this may refer to the generation that reached maturity and then died during the forty-year punishment, or to the general lifespan described in Psalm 90, which is attributed to Moses during this period.</note>

### Joshua lived forty years beyond seventy.

The oldest of the *Israelites* who died in the wilderness lived no longer than seventy years; those who outlived that time did not truly live but languished <note>original: "languish"; a reference to Psalm 90:10, which suggests that years beyond seventy are filled with "labor and sorrow."</note>. That curse continued until it had devoured that entire generation included in the sentence of death, which was thoroughly executed within the forty years of their dwelling in the wilderness. *Joshua* outlived them and brought their children into the Land of *Canaan*.

In total, he lived as long as the oldest among them—namely, seventy years—and yet he lived as many years more as they had wandered in the wilderness, which was forty years. For the days of his life were 110 years, which are seventy and forty. The forty years he had above the ordinary age of the others were given to him as an addition of as much time as God had taken from the others.

<summary>This page explains that while the generation of Israelites cursed to die in the wilderness lived to approximately seventy years, Joshua was granted an additional forty years—matching the time spent wandering—to reach the age of 110.</summary>

<page-num>[ 77 ]</page-num>

### God doubles the days of a man.

### After the complainers were condemned to die in the wilderness, Joshua lived just as many years as he had lived up to that point.

When God pronounced the sentence against those who had complained <note>original: "murmured"; referring to the Israelites who rebelled against God's plan to enter the Promised Land (Numbers 14).</note>, *Joshua* was fifty-five years of age, and he lived for fifty-five years more, for he died in his hundred and tenth year.

The proof of it is evident. The condemnation of the complainers was pronounced in the second year after the departure from *Egypt*. From that time until *Joshua* entered *Canaan*, thirty-eight years passed; when these are added to the seventeen years that *Joshua* lived afterward, they total fifty-five years. Now, since *Joshua* was 110 years of age when he died, he must have been fifty-five years old when the others were condemned to die in the wilderness. That *Joshua* lived seventeen years after his entry into *Canaan* is beyond dispute.

Thus the life of that excellent man

### Four Miracles God Performed in the Conquest of Canaan:
### 1. In the Water. 2. In the Earth. 3. In the Air. 4. In the Heavens.

1. In the **Water**, when he made the River **Jordan** stand still—and even run backward—simply at the presence of the Ark <note>referring to the Ark of the Covenant, the sacred chest containing the tablets of the Ten Commandments.</note>.

2. In the **Earth**, when he threw down the walls of **Jericho** simply at the sound of the ram’s horns.

3. In the **Air**, when he killed his enemies with the hail that fell upon them like stones (Joshua 10:11).

4. In the **Heavens**, when he stopped the sun and the moon in their paths.

These wonders happened in succession, and in

But those miracles showed them that the God of the Hebrews had universal command over everything: in the water, on the earth, in the air, and in heaven.

Even the second of the first two was in some way less grand <note>original: "illustrious"</note> than the first, just as a river is smaller than the sea.

Once God's wisdom has sufficiently shown His power through one or two acts of a certain kind, He takes delight in making His miracles less spectacular. He does this so that people might believe in Him for His smallest works as much as for His greatest.

### A progression of four methods that occurred in succession during the Conquest of the cities of Canaan: the first was achieved by miracle, the second by strategy <note>original: "stratagem"</note>, the third by voluntary surrender, and the fourth and those following by force.

The most excellent way to achieve a conquest is through the direct and miraculous work of God. That which comes directly from Him—even the destruction <note>original: "the evil itself"; in this context, the author refers to the calamity of war as a divine judgment</note>—is always preferable to that which happens through the hands of men.

The second method—inferior to the first but more noble than the two that follow—is when victory is obtained through diligence and wisdom <note>original: "industry and prudence"</note>, which are the most commendable tools <note>original: "arms"</note> of humanity.

The third method is when the enemy, lacking courage, submits without striking a blow.

The fourth method is through the strength of the arm and of the sword.

| :--- | :--- |
| **Canaan** | The region west of the Jordan River, promised to the Israelites. |
| **Conquest** | The act of taking control of a territory through military force. |
| **Miracle** | An event that contradicts known laws of nature, attributed to God. |
| **Stratagem** | A clever plot or tactic used to deceive or outmaneuver an enemy. |
| **Surrender** | To give up or yield to the power or control of another. |
| **Force** | The use of physical or military power to achieve an objective. |
| **Prudence** | The quality of being wise, cautious, and practical. |

God: Was there not a man in all *Israel* worthy of that honor except for the son of a prostitute? The *Israelites* were prone to idolatry, which is a form of spiritual fornication; therefore, God raised an enemy against them. They begged for pardon and prayed to God to deliver them. God granted their prayer, but He intended for them to endure the shame of being delivered by a man conceived in fornication—and for that man to exercise authority over them from then on. His purpose was to rebuke them for their idolatry and to show them that, since they were disgraceful themselves because of their spiritual fornication, they deserved to be commanded by the son of a prostitute <note>original: "harlot"</note>.

### An interval of many months, during which *David* composed no Psalm.

### Why God, having pardoned David, would never grant him the life of the child he had with Bathsheba.

We know with what intensity *David* prayed and desired the life of that child, but he could never obtain it.

We should observe here an effect of God's wisdom. It might have been uncertain whether that child was *David's* or not, for the timing of his birth showed that he was conceived <note>original: "begotten"</note> while *Uriah*, the first husband of *Bathsheba*, was still alive. Had that child lived, being the first son of *David* and *Bathsheba*, he might have succeeded *David* to the kingdom as well as *Solomon*, whom they had later. But just as his paternity <note>original: "Birth"</note> might have been disputed, so his title to the throne might have been questioned, and it would have caused great trouble in the royal succession.

And even if no doubt had been made regarding his being the son of *David*, because he was conceived in adultery, the law made him ineligible for the crown. He might have had some supporters <note>original: "Partizans"</note>, but he would certainly have faced competitors armed with strong arguments, and that would have caused...

But there is an even higher and more important reason why it did not please God for that child to live. *Christ* was to descend from *David* and *Bathsheba*—not, in fact, through their eldest son, for our Lord descended from *Nathan* <note>Nathan was another son of David and Bathsheba; he is listed in the genealogy of Jesus found in the Gospel of Luke</note>, who was only a younger brother. For this reason, *St. Matthew*, in his first chapter, when naming the ancestors of *Christ*, shows him descending from *Solomon*. Now, it was not appropriate that *Christ* should succeed, either by blood <note>original: "naturally"</note> or by law, a person whose descent from *David* or status as a lawful successor might be doubted. For this reason, God was pleased that the child should die (even though he was the firstborn), and specifically on the seventh day after his birth <note>original: "nativity"</note>.

### *The seven days of the child of* David, *and the seven days of the Reign of* Zimri, 1 Kings 16:15.

These two individuals have no relation to each other, nor anything in common; yet the life of the first and the reign of the last shared the exact same span of time. Short as it was, it has been recorded in Sacred History <note>The author refers to the Bible as "Sacred History," emphasizing its role as a factual record of divine providence</note>. The chronological gaps between these examples may contain many secrets unknown to us. Certainly, none of the Kings of Judah had a reign as short as this King of Israel <note>Zimri, a chariot commander who assassinated King Elah to take the throne, only to be overthrown seven days later</note>, who held the throne for only as many days as David’s child lived. Furthermore, the number seven is particularly noteworthy here.

### Solomon's fleet spent three whole years on a voyage that might have been completed in less than one year; it is very likely that for lack of a sea compass, that voyage... <note>In the 17th century, it was a common scholarly debate whether ancient navigators possessed the "mariner's compass" or if their long voyages were due to the need to hug the coastline</note>

<meta>This page continues the inquiry into King Solomon's naval expeditions, explaining why they took three years and why the technology of the magnetic compass was withheld from the Israelites.</meta>

<page-num>91</page-num>

### Why the voyage was so long, and why that secret was not discovered by Solomon.

His ships sailed along the *Mediterranean Sea*, and so entered the main ocean around *Africa*. At the present day, the journey there and back would not take so much time. At that time, they were ignorant of how to use a needle touched by a loadstone <note>original: "Load-stone"; a piece of magnetite used to magnetize iron needles for early compasses</note>. Solomon himself, as great a naturalist <note>A "naturalist" in this context refers to a scholar of the natural sciences</note> as he was, did not know of it, and God was not pleased to let him know it, for many reasons.

First, God did not want his people to apply themselves too much to navigation, lest *Israel* should be corrupted by trade with other nations. Therefore, of all the Tribes, only three—namely *Zebulun*, *Issachar*, and *Asher* <note>original: "Zabulon, Issacher, and Asser"</note>—had their homes by the seaside.

He also did not want them to go far from the land of *Canaan* for many significant reasons.

Moreover, as for that secret of nature, God reserved it for later times for the discovery of the New World, which required longer voyages and, consequently, a more specific and constant skill than that used in ancient times <note>original: "the Antiquity"</note>.

<summary>This page explains that Solomon's long voyages were due to the lack of a magnetic compass, a technology the author argues was divinely withheld to keep Israel from being corrupted by foreign commerce and to reserve the discovery of the Americas for a later era.</summary>

<keywords>Solomon, navigation, Mediterranean Sea, Africa, Loadstone, compass, Israel, Zebulun, Issachar, Asher, Canaan, New World</keywords>

*The Temple had stood thirty years after it was finished when the Ten Tribes forsook it. These ten tribes, who abandoned the Temple at the suggestion of *Jeroboam* <note>Jeroboam was the first king of the breakaway Northern Kingdom of Israel; he set up alternative golden calf shrines to prevent his people from traveling to the Temple in Jerusalem</note>, did as much as they possibly could to annul its holiness. This was essentially the same as if they had destroyed the building itself. Their desertion happened in the thirtieth year after the Temple was finished; this is clear to anyone who calculates the years of the reign of *Solomon*, followed by the revolt of the ten tribes <note>The "Ten Tribes" refers to the northern kingdom of Israel, which split from the southern kingdom of Judah after Solomon's death</note>.

Similarly, Jesus Christ was also in the thirtieth year of his age when, while in the Temple, he said to the Jews, "*Destroy this temple*" (John 2:19). The chronology of the New Testament shows it was a few months after Jesus Christ had entered the thirtieth year of his age (Luke 3:23). ...many ages the *Resurrection* of all those who have been raised from the Dead.

In the persons of those who were raised from the dead, and of those who were bodily translated into Heaven, God has shown the previews <note>original: "Prologues"</note> of our future Resurrection and of the ascension <note>original: "transportation"</note> of our bodies that are to be taken up into Heaven. Afterwards, while death continued to reign for a long time, God showed that the resurrection of our bodies is a necessary precursor to their ascension into heaven. Since then, God has continued the miracle of the resurrection at certain times until the Ascension of Christ, and for a short time after that as well. 

But the fact remains that the resurrection of the first person ever raised from the dead was soon followed by a miraculous Ascension—specifically, the ascension of the very man through whom that dead person was raised. One cannot object that these two wonders were not performed on one and the same person, for that particular miracle was reserved for the days of Christ. Until Christ raised Himself, no one could ever be raised from the dead specifically to then ascend bodily into Heaven.

### The resurrection of a person from the dead was never the first miracle performed by any man, not even by Christ himself. The reason that can be given for this.

No one has ever raised anyone from the dead without first having performed <note>original: "wrought"</note> miracles of another kind.

Before *Elijah* <note>original: "Elija"</note> raised the son of the widow of *Zarephath* <note>original: "Sarepta"</note>, he had stopped the rain and the dew, preventing it from falling on the ground for a period <note>original: "by the space"</note> of three years and six months; he had also multiplied the oil and the flour <note>original: "Meal"</note> of the widow.

Before *Elisha* raised the son of the Shunammite woman, he had divided the waters of the *Jordan* River, multiplied the loaves of bread, etc.

Before Saint *Peter* raised *Tabitha*, he had cured a man who was lame from birth, and one who was sick with paralysis <note>original: "Palsey"</note> (Acts 3, 9).

Before Saint *Paul* raised *Eutychus* <note>original: "Eutichus"</note>, he had struck the impostor *Elymas* with blindness, cured one who was born lame, and cast out a spirit of divination <note>original: "spirit of Python"; a reference to the Pythian spirit of the Delphic oracle</note> (Acts 13, 14, and 16).

# performed outside <note>original: "wrought out of"</note> the Land of Israel.

The first was performed <note>original: "wrought"</note> in *Sarepta* of *Sidon* by the Prophet *Elijah*, who raised the son of the widow from the dead. The last was performed in *Troas*, a city of Asia Minor <note>original: "the lesser Asia"</note>, by St. *Paul*, who raised *Eutychus* from the dead. Both of these took place in the land of the Gentiles <note>original: "the Heathen"</note>.

In the first place, God has shown that his power over the dead was not limited to the land of *Israel*. Moreover, he showed that the Resurrection of the time to come was not only for the *Jew*, or for any other *Israelite*, but for the *Gentiles* also; for the *Jews* believed it belonged only to them and to their proselytes <term>proselyte: a person who has converted from one religion or belief system to another</term>.

But God was chiefly pleased to show the *Gentiles* that which they believed to be impossible. The resurrection of the dead seemed to them contrary to all reason; the philosophers mocked St. *Paul* when he preached it, as that doctrine was ridiculous to all the *Gentiles*. Now, so that they might be convinced of its possibility, God was pleased that they should see that miracle even in their own land—indeed, it was meant both to begin and end there.

### *If the sun had leaped ten degrees forward, according to the offer that was made, the question is: By how much would its speed have then surpassed the speed of its ordinary motion?*

Hezekiah <note>original: "HEzekia"</note> did not accept that offer, but required that the sun might go back, which it did. Many hold that this backward movement was done in a single moment, but that is a matter of dispute. It is more certain that if the sun had leaped forward by as many degrees as God offered to his choice, it would have occurred in a shorter time than that luminary <note>original: "Luminary"; a term used for a celestial body that gives off light, such as the sun or moon</note> ordinarily employs in traveling so long a distance. For what miracle would it have been if the sun, moving forward and following its ordinary course from east to west, had not traveled faster than it usually did?

<summary>The author concludes a chronological analysis of the Jewish state and begins a technical discussion of the miracle where the sun moved backward on Hezekiah's sundial, debating the mechanics of its speed.</summary>
<keywords>Canaan, Hezekiah, Hezekiah's Sundial, solar motion, biblical chronology, miracles</keywords>

--- Page 140 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical investigation into the miracle of Hezekiah’s sundial, specifically analyzing the physical distance and time implied by the "ten degrees" mentioned in the biblical account.</meta>

<page-num>106</page-num>

Now, to understand how great this speed would have been, we must first see what the measurements were of the ten degrees mentioned in that story. If they were ten hours, as the Jews believe, they would have been 150 degrees on the equinoctial line <note>original: "Æquinoctial line"; the celestial equator, used here to measure the sun's path</note>—calculated at fifteen degrees for every one hour. This is a very long distance, which would have required about five-sixths of the time the sun spends in one natural day.

If they were only half-hours, as most of our interpreters believe, the sun would still have had to move faster than usual for a period of five hours.

But a modern writer significantly shortens this duration, believing the ten degrees were those which we calculate on the equinoctial line as two-thirds of an hour. And so, according to his opinion, the sun would have moved back by only two-thirds of an hour. Likewise, when God offered the alternative—to cause the sun to hasten ten degrees forward—it would have been by no more than two-thirds of an hour.

Now, even if we accept this interpretation, which is the smallest measure that can be applied to that story, we would still find it to be a prodigious wonder.

But we must also see how much less than those two-thirds of an hour the sun would have spent in traveling that distance.

<summary>The author analyzes different interpretations of the "ten degrees" on Hezekiah's sundial, comparing theories that they represented ten hours, five hours, or only forty minutes (two-thirds of an hour).</summary>
<keywords>Hezekiah's Sundial, ten degrees, astronomical measurement, equinoctial line, biblical interpretation, solar motion</keywords>

--- Page 141 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical debate regarding the miracle of Hezekiah’s sundial, specifically focusing on the physical mechanics and speed required for the sun to move "ten degrees" in a very short amount of time.</meta>

<page-num>107</page-num>

The most common opinion is that the sun must have leaped in a single moment. This is to say that, in an instant, it covered as much ground <note>original: "made as much riddance of his way"</note> as it usually does in forty minutes (two-thirds of an hour). Certainly, the miracle that God provided consisted of this: that the sun completed its path in the shortest time possible.

Because of this, philosophers <note>original: "Philosophers"; referring here to natural philosophers or scientists of the era</note> would deny that local motion <term>local motion</term> <note>The physical movement of an object from one place to another in space.</note> can occur in a single moment. Even if we grant that point, the speed of even the longest movement can be increased if God impels a body to such a degree that the motion is finished in a span of time almost imperceptible because of its shortness.

Instead of a single moment, let us suppose the sun spent one minute leaping forward. Since its distance traveled was equivalent to two-thirds of an hour, in that one minute it would have traveled as far as it naturally does in forty minutes (which is two-thirds of an hour). Thus, its speed would have been forty times greater than its ordinary motion. Furthermore, let us suppose it had taken ten minutes to leap forward; in that case, its motion would have been four times faster than it is normally.

Now, it is known by demonstration <note>original: "demonstration"; a logical or mathematical proof</note> that in one minute the sun normally travels...

This is a prodigious wonder.

### Why God did not offer to Hezekiah to cause the sun to stand still, as he did for Joshua, but instead to make it move extraordinarily.

God has often performed <note>original: "wrought"</note> miracles through several creatures of the same kind. For example, He has raised many from the dead, cured many of leprosy, and so on. But regarding the motion of the celestial bodies <term>celestial bodies</term> <note>Objects in space such as the sun, moon, and stars.</note>, He was never pleased to show more than two miracles: one by stopping them <note>A reference to the biblical account in Joshua 10:12–13, where the sun stood still.</note> and another time by changing their motion <note>A reference to Hezekiah's sign in 2 Kings 20, where the shadow moved backward.</note>. This was undoubtedly so that the general order of the world, which depends on the movement of the heavens, would not be frequently interrupted.

<summary>The author describes the incredible speed required for the sun to move during Hezekiah's miracle and explains that God rarely alters the celestial bodies to prevent disrupting the natural order of the universe.</summary>
<keywords>Hezekiah, Joshua, miracles, celestial bodies, German leagues, providence, astronomy, natural order</keywords>
