Therefore, He did not repeat the first miracle but was pleased that the second should differ entirely—indeed, it was even the opposite of the first.

The Jews had many prophets, such as *Haggai*, *Zechariah*, and *Malachi*, and other holy writers such as *Esdras* <note>original: "Esdras"; the Latin form of Ezra</note> and *Nehemiah*. However, they experienced no more miracles until the time of the New Testament drew near.

In all ages of the Church, prophecy has always lasted longer than miracles. Between the Creation and the Flood, a span of 1,656 years, among the many divine inspirations the first patriarchs received, there was only ever one miracle: the translation <note>original: "translation"; the bodily taking of Enoch into heaven without his dying</note> of *Enoch*, 987 years after the creation of the world. But prophecy (being more necessary than miracles) has been more frequent and of a longer duration.

### The dangerous assertions of many famous interpreters who think that Psalm 74 was composed about the persecution of Antiochus.

The previous observation leads us to this point. All the parts of the Old Testament, as well as those of the New, came from divine inspiration. Such inspiration is not found to have existed in anyone from the time of Malachi and Nehemiah <note>Malachi and Nehemiah are traditionally considered the final voices of the Old Testament era, roughly 400 years before the birth of Jesus.</note> until the New Testament; writings composed during that interval can only be considered the works of men. And this is one of our arguments against the Apocrypha <note>A group of books included in some versions of the Bible but rejected by others because they were written after the age of the Hebrew prophets.</note>. Now, the persecution by Antiochus <note>Antiochus IV Epiphanes (reigned 175–164 BCE), a king who famously attempted to suppress Jewish customs, leading to the Maccabean Revolt.</note> did not occur until more than a hundred years after Malachi and Nehemiah had finished the final writings of the Old Testament. How then can a writing from such a late date have a place in the canonical Scripture? This Psalm is much older than the time of Antiochus, despite all the arguments that might be alleged to the contrary.

<summary>The author argues that Psalm 74 must predate the persecution of Antiochus IV because divine inspiration supposedly ceased between the time of Malachi and the New Testament. This serves as a theological defense for the fixed boundaries of the biblical canon against later additions like the Apocrypha.</summary>

### *The remarkable arrangement of God in the distribution of the empires shown to Daniel, and successively divided among the offspring of the three sons of Noah.*

The first of those empires—namely, that of *Babylon*—was given to the descendants of *Ham* <note>original: "Cham"</note>. *Nimrod*, descending from *Ham*, was its founder (*Genesis* 10:6, 8, 10).

The second empire—that of the *Medes* and *Persians*—was held jointly by the descendants of *Japheth* and *Shem* <note>original: "Sem"</note>. This is because *Elam* <note>original: "Helam"</note>, from whom the *Persians* descended, was the son of *Shem*; and *Madai* <note>The ancestor of the Medes according to the Table of Nations.</note> was the son of *Japheth* (*Genesis* 10:2, 22).

The empire of the *Greeks* <note>original: "Grecians"</note> and that of the *Romans* were wholly possessed by the race of *Japheth*, whose genealogy is found in *Genesis* 10. Let the reader understand this.

It should be observed that in the distribution of these empires, God has kept an order quite contrary to the birth order of the three sons of *Noah*; for

### *Diverse Measures and Proportions of Time Given to the Empires of Babylon, Persia, and Greece.*

A certain length of time was assigned to each of them (Daniel 7:12), but the durations <note>original: "measures"</note> were different and varied in proportion.

The Empire of *Babylon* lasted for 70 years, which was also the length <note>original: "term"</note> of the Jewish Captivity. That period of 70 years was designated to fulfill the "Sabbaths of the Land" (2 Chronicles 36:21). <note>The "Sabbaths of the Land" refers to the biblical law in Leviticus requiring the land to lie fallow every seventh year. The 70-year exile was traditionally interpreted as "repayment" for the years the Israelites failed to observe this rest.</note> The Empire of the *Persians* continued for 130 years, which was 60 years longer than the duration of *Babylon*. It was necessary for the empire under which the Temple was to be rebuilt—and which was to achieve other long-term goals concerning the Church of God—to last longer than the previous one. Therefore, it exceeded the former empire by 60 years.

However, for several other significant <note>original: "weighty"</note> reasons, the *Grecian* Empire lasted for five times 60 years <note>totaling 300 years</note>, which ended before the death of Christ. For from the time *Alexander* the Great conquered the *Persians* until *Egypt*, the last remnant of the *Grecian* Empire, was incor- <note>The text cuts off here; the catchword "incor-" likely begins the word "incorporated," referring to Egypt's annexation by the Roman Empire.</note>

<summary>The author compares the lifespans of the Babylonian (70 years), Persian (130 years), and Greek (300 years) Empires, arguing that each was granted a specific duration by God to fulfill different historical and religious purposes.</summary>

### *From the conclusion of the history of the Old Testament to the destruction of the Second Temple (which marked the end of its religious services), one-tenth of the age of the world passed after the tenth prophetic year.*

We have seen that the history of the Old Testament ended when the Kingdom of the *Persians* was nearing its end <note>original: "hastening to its period"</note>; this occurred in the year 3600, which was also the end of the tenth prophetic year. Now, from that time to the destruction of the Second Temple in the year 4000 of the world's history <note>This follows an *Anno Mundi* (Year of the World) chronology, a system used by early modern scholars to date events from the biblical Creation.</note>, 400 years passed. This was one-tenth of the total age the world had reached since the Creation. 

And because that destruction happened forty years after the death of *Christ*, we see that these forty years were one-tenth of the 400 years that had passed since the history of the Old Testament was finished—just as those 400 years were one-tenth of the 4000 years completed at the ruin of the Second Temple.

<summary>This page calculates the chronological proportions of world history, noting that the 400 years between the Old Testament's end and the Second Temple's destruction represent exactly one-tenth of the 4,000 years since Creation.</summary>
<keywords>chronology, Second Temple, Old Testament, Persian Empire, Prophetic year, Creation, Jesus Christ, biblical history, 70 AD</keywords>

# A Supplement of many Historical Observations, included in those previously mentioned, and arranged according to the order of time.

### *A Speculation on why Scripture does not name the time—namely the year or the day—when Sin began.*

It is in vain for men to try to determine how much time passed from the creation of the angels to the revolt of many of them, or how long *Adam* remained in the state of innocence <note>original: "Innocency"; this refers to the period in Eden before the Fall of Man</note>. It is not mentioned on what day, nor at what hour of the day, nor even in what year sin entered the world. It is very likely, and based on very strong reasons, that *Adam* continued

<summary>This page introduces a series of supplemental historical observations, specifically speculating on why the Bible does not record the exact date or time of the fall of the angels or the beginning of human sin.</summary>
<keywords>Adam, Fall of Man, Creation of Angels, innocence, original sin, biblical chronology, historical observations</keywords>

--- Page 160 ---
<meta>This page continues the speculation on the timing of the Fall of Man, picking up from the previous page's discussion of Adam's time in Eden. It focuses on the theological reasons why Scripture excludes the specific dates of sinful acts while documenting the timing of God's creative acts.</meta>

...continued for only a very few days in his original state of perfection <note>original: "primitive integrity"</note>. Yet the biblical history does not name the day or the year of his fall, nor in what age it occurred.

Just as Scripture never records the total lifespan of a wicked man—as many have observed—it also does not mark the time when sin was born. Many sins committed since then are described with specific details about when they happened; however, the first sin, which produced all the others, is mentioned without any indication of when it began.

All the speculation we can offer regarding this is as follows: when Scripture relates the Creation of God's works, it distinguishes them from those things that were not of His making, even though they happened soon after the Creation. One of the signs of this distinction is that for all of God's works, it is recorded on which day they began to exist; but the day sin was born is not marked in the calendar <note>original: "Kalendar"</note> of the Holy Spirit <note>original: "holy Ghost"</note>.

Indeed, long after the Creation, Scripture also declined to state the year in which *Cain* killed his brother. That silence has many causes, but we can perceive only a very few of them.

<summary>The author argues that the Bible intentionally omits the dates of sinful events, such as the Fall of Adam or Cain's murder of Abel, to distinguish human corruption from God's orderly acts of creation.</summary>
<keywords>Adam, Fall of Man, original sin, biblical chronology, Cain, Creation, Holy Spirit, providence</keywords>

--- Page 161 ---
<meta>This page transitions from the discussion of the timing of the Fall to a specific critique of how angels are depicted in biblical history, challenging the popular assumption that they always appeared with wings.</meta>

### *A common misconception <note>original: "vulgar prejudice"</note> of those who think that angels appeared with wings whenever they were seen by men.*

They were represented this way [with wings] on the Mercy Seat <note>original: "Propitiatory," referring to the golden cover of the Ark of the Covenant adorned with cherubim</note>, and they may have appeared that way to the eyes of *Adam* when they were stationed at the entrances <note>original: "Avenues"</note> of the Garden of Eden to prevent him from returning. However, when they appeared to *Abraham* and *Lot* in *Sodom*—who both offered them food—they certainly did not appear in the form of men with wings. If they had, they would not have been mistaken for real men, yet those Patriarchs clearly believed them to be human. These biblical stories and many others are commonly misrepresented <note>original: "falsified"</note> in *paintings*. Because the common imagination is already biased <note>original: "prepossessed"</note>, it is further reinforced in this misunderstanding.

<summary>The author argues against the popular belief that angels always appeared with wings, noting that in several biblical accounts, such as those of Abraham and Lot, angels were mistaken for ordinary men.</summary>
<keywords>angels, wings, iconography, Abraham, Lot, Sodom, Adam, Mercy Seat, biblical interpretation, religious art</keywords>

--- Page 162 ---
<meta>This page transitions from a discussion about the physical appearance of angels to a reflection on how God communicates judgment to humanity, highlighting a shift from direct speech to the use of prophetic messengers.</meta>

### Since the judgment given against Cain, God has never directly <note>original: "immediately"</note> pronounced the sentence of condemnation to any sinner, except for only one.

To the first three offenders that ever existed—the *Serpent*, *Eve*, and *Adam*—God himself pronounced the sentence. He did the same for the next offender after them, namely *Cain*. But since then, although he has condemned many transgressors and decreed the sentence, he never pronounced it to them with his own mouth, except to only one person mentioned in 1 *Kings* 13:20 <note>This refers to the "man of God" from Judah who was judged for his disobedience</note>. To all others, he usually <note>original: "ordinarily"</note> caused it to be pronounced by some prophet acting as a messenger of his justice, of which there are many examples.

Without a doubt, this is to show us that he is much more inclined toward mercy than toward severity <note>original: "rigour"</note>, even when he exercises justice. It is as if he would have judgment pronounced in his absence, as it were, while he withdraws out of pity.

<summary>The author observes that after the initial judgments of Adam, Eve, and Cain, God ceased pronouncing sentences of condemnation personally, instead using prophets to deliver His justice as a sign of His preference for mercy.</summary>
<keywords>Cain, Adam, Eve, judgment, condemnation, 1 Kings 13, mercy, justice, prophets, divine communication</keywords>

--- Page 163 ---
<meta>This page transitions from a discussion about the divine method of pronouncing judgment via prophets to the specific historical instances where God demonstrated the reality of the afterlife through bodily ascensions during times of extreme skepticism.</meta>

### Concerning the Three Distinct Times of the Ascension of Enoch, Elijah, and Christ.

At each of these three times, a notable impiety <note>original: "impiety," meaning a lack of religious reverence or godlessness</note> reigned, which stifled the belief in the life to come. In the days of Enoch, atheism was almost universal throughout the whole world. In the time of Elijah, the worship of Baal <note>original: "Baalism"</note> dominated in Israel. And when Christ was on earth, the philosophy of the Sadducees <note>original: "Sadducism"</note>—which denied the immortality of the soul and the resurrection of the body—was in fashion among the Jews.

To refute <note>original: "convince"</note> those godless beliefs and to confirm the faith in the life to come, God gave a visible example of it at each of these three times.

---

### The History of the Eras <note>original: "Times"</note> in Which Angels Have Appeared

We insert this topic here because the first time angels spoke to men was in the time of Abraham, which is the period we are currently discussing. This is the chronological order of their appearances <note>original: "apparitions"</note>:

1. After the Cherubim <note>original: "Cherubins"</note>—who were placed at the entrance of the Garden of Eden—angels did not appear to men for over two hundred years. In the days of Abraham, they began to appear once again.

2. From that time onward, they appeared from

<meta>This page continues the chronological timeline of angelic appearances, picking up from the period starting with Abraham.</meta>

time to time for a period of 204 years, until *Jacob* went down into *Egypt*.

3. After that, for 215 years, they were not seen until the vision of the burning bush, where the angel appeared to *Moses*.

4. Afterward, their appearances <note>original: "Apparitions"</note> were very frequent and continued until the writing of the Old Testament was almost finished. The last person in the Old Testament to whom the angels appeared was the prophet *Zechariah* <note>original: "Zacharia"</note>.

5. From that time on, there was a cessation of their appearances for over 400 years until the beginning of the New Testament, when an angel appeared to *Zechariah* <note>original: "Zachary"</note>, the father of *John the Baptist*.

From that time, the angels continued their appearances and were seen by many people on several occasions—such as by the *Virgin* Mary, *Joseph*, and the *Apostles*—until the New Testament was completed.

But since the Scripture was fully completed <note>original: "totally perfected"</note>, the angels have appeared no more. The last angel to appear was the one who dictated the *Revelation* to *Saint John* <note>original: "S. John"</note>.

### *The oldest trade that we read about has been the buying and selling of human beings.*

In Genesis 17:12, it speaks of servants bought with the price of silver. Of all types of commerce, this is the first mentioned in the Bible <note>original: "Scripture"</note>. If it was not established before all others, it is at least very ancient, and was used shortly after Canaan was condemned to slavery <note>Canaan was the grandson of Noah; according to Genesis 9:25, he was cursed to be a "servant of servants" to his brothers.</note>.

When God is willing to use spirits to afflict men, He employs bad angels to discipline <note>original: "chastise"</note> good men, and on the contrary, good angels to punish bad men. ### Why the Scripture Records the Ridiculous Agreement Between Rachel and Leah (Genesis 30:14)

When *Leah* made that deal <note>original: "bargain." This refers to Leah "hiring" Jacob's company for the night from Rachel in exchange for mandrakes.</note>, she already had four sons. The last of these was *Judah*, from whom Christ was eventually to come. If she had known that she already had a son who, in his physical lineage <note>original: "according to the flesh"</note>, was to be the forefather of the Savior, would she have been jealous of *Rachel*? The Scripture records this story (which would otherwise seem trivial <note>original: "frivolous"</note>) to show, as stated previously, that salvation does not come from the wisdom or the foresight of Christ's ancestors. This argument is specifically directed against the *Jews*, who believe they will be saved by the merits of the Patriarchs <note>This refers to the concept of *Zechut Avot*, or "the merit of the fathers," the idea that the righteousness of ancestors brings favor to their descendants.</note>.

Sins that men do not think they are responsible for punishing, God punishes himself—and for the most part, immediately. belonging to the *Ceremonial Law*, should be lost.

Most of the *Hebrew* names expressing them are like riddles to us—indeed, even to the *Jews* themselves. We do not know what kind of cedar was used for the timber of the Tabernacle, nor the specific shade of purple used for the Tabernacle's curtains (the method of making it is now lost). We also do not know all the precious stones set in the breastplate of the High Priest. We do not know the shapes of the musical instruments on which they sang their sacred hymns, nor whether their *phylacteries* <note>original: "Philactaries"; here referring to the ritual items or decorative fringes worn by the Israelites</note> were fringes, bands, or borders. We do not know the color of the cord or string—whether it was purple, sea-green, or sky-blue—nor the ingredients of the mixture <note>original: "Confection"</note> with which the priests were anointed. Many other things are also obscure in their terminology <note>original: "nomenclature"</note>, and their interpretations are just as uncertain; yet all these words were very easy to understand when God first caused them to be written and spoken.

However, His intention was to abolish the *Mosaic ceremonies* <note>original: "Mosaical Ceremonies"</note> after a certain period of time. Because they were to be suppressed, He caused their meaning <note>original: "signification"</note> to be lost so that no one could re-establish the practice of them.

<summary>This page argues that God intentionally allowed the specific meanings of Hebrew terms for Tabernacle materials and rituals to be lost to ensure that the Mosaic ceremonial laws could not be revived after their appointed time.</summary>

### The Tables of the Law were shut up in the Ark, and no one had the power to see them: What, then, was the use of them?

If they had been exposed to public view, people would have distracted <note>original: "amused"; in this context meaning to divert one's attention or occupy oneself idly</note> themselves by looking at the physical craftsmanship instead of searching for the meaning of it. The Law is spiritual and should be viewed with spiritual eyes. But just as *Moses* put a veil over his face so that the children of *Israel* could not look at the end of the Law (2 *Corinthians* 3:13), it seems that for the same reason, the Tables were hidden from them. What use, then, did they have for them? They were a pledge <note>original: "earnest"</note> of the presence of God among them. How did they know that those Tables, shut inside the Ark, were the work of God? Because God miraculously

### The Miracle that lasted the longest

It was the preservation of the *manna*, which was set aside and kept in a golden pot (Exodus 16:33 and Hebrews 9:4). *Manna*—with the exception of that which was gathered for the Sabbath day—would spoil <note>original: "corrupt"</note> the very next day and breed worms. But the portion of that food which God commanded to be kept so that future generations <note>original: "posterity"</note> might look upon it, continued for many ages without the slightest decay. We cannot say exactly how long it lasted; however, even if it had been stored away near the end of the Israelites' final year in the wilderness and lasted only as long as the Tabernacle was in use—that is <note>original: "viz."</note>, until the first Temple was finished—it would have been 447 years. No other miracle ever lasted so long.

<summary>The author describes the divine protection of the Ark through the punishments of the Beth-shemites and Uzzah, and identifies the 447-year preservation of the manna as the longest-lasting miracle in biblical history.</summary>

### *In Israel, no one was ever condemned to the penalty of banishment. The errors of many interpreters regarding certain terms of the Law.*

Regarding certain kinds of sins, the Law says that the person found guilty *shall be cut off from his people* (such as one who neglected circumcision) or *cut off from the assembly of Israel* (such as one who, during the time of the Passover, ate any leavened bread), and similar phrases concerning many other violations or omissions of the ceremonial laws <note>original: "Ceremonial Ordinances"</note>. 

However, the terms of that penal law are interpreted in different ways. Many scholars <note>original: "Expositors"</note> believe that these words signify banishment. But: 

1. A man might be "cut off from the assembly" even though he still lived in the land of *Israel*. For instance, the person of illegitimate birth <note>original: "Bastard"; in this context, the Hebrew term 'mamzer' (Deuteronomy 23:2), referring to one born of an forbidden relationship</note>, the *Moabite*, and the *Ammonite* could dwell in the land of *Israel*, and yet they were excluded from the assembly. 

2. In the entire history of the Old Testament, there is not one example found of any *Israelite* who was legally banished from his country for any offense whatsoever; there

<summary>The author argues that the biblical phrase "cut off from his people" does not mean banishment, noting that certain groups lived in Israel while being excluded from the assembly, and that there are no historical examples of judicial banishment in the Old Testament.</summary>

3. 4. Although the Lawgiver <note>referring to God as the source of the Mosaic Law</note> imposed worldly <note>original: "temporal"</note> punishments on offenders <note>original: "Delinquents"</note>, he still cared for their souls and would not expose them to the danger of becoming idolaters. Consider what danger a man would have been in if he had been forced to live among idolaters, far from the public religious services <note>original: "Exercises"</note> and the holy teachings that could only be found in the land of *Israel*.

Sometimes God commanded his children to go and live for a time in the land of unbelievers <note>original: "Infidels"</note>, as he did with the *Shunammite* woman and her son (2 Kings 8) and later with *Joseph* and the *Virgin* Mary. But: 

1. There is a great difference between a place of exile and a place of refuge; in the one, a man is subjected to forced relocation <note>original: "constraint"</note>; in the other, he enjoys his liberty. 

2. When God sent people into foreign countries, he preserved them from the corrupting influence <note>original: "contagion"</note> of idolaters through his special assistance, as

And even when God has transported the whole nation and caused them to live among unbelievers <note>original: "Infidels"</note>—as *Israel* in *Egypt*, the ten tribes in *Assyria*, and the *Jews* in *Babylon*—and many of them were corrupted with idolatry, it was an act of His justice to punish their former idolatry by confining them to the land of idols, where they filled the measure of their offensive practices <note>original: "abominations"</note>. However, He has not granted that authority <note>original: "jurisdiction"</note> into the hands of men. As for the faithful who were overwhelmed in the general disaster, He provided them with protections <note>original: "Antidotes"</note> against the infections of paganism, as He did for *Daniel* and his companions. This is a result of His grace, which men cannot give; therefore, He gave men no authority to expose the souls of others to such manifest danger as might have been caused by banishment.

<meta>This page continues the discussion of 2 Samuel 24, focusing on the specific nature of the plague (pestilence) sent as a punishment for David’s census and comparing divine judgments to man-made calamities like war.</meta>

<margin>2 Samuel 24</margin> Scripture does not mention any other plague among the *Israelites*. Among public punishments <note>original: "scourges"</note>, this is the least frequent, unless it occurs in places where the quality of the air, the density of the population, and other circumstances <note>original: "accidents"</note> cause a high death toll. Sometimes, as with the one God sent upon *Israel*, it proceeds directly from God. However, the evils or calamities that come directly from Him are never as frequent, as widespread, or as long-lasting as those where human actions intervene—such as war, about which many excellent observations could be made.

The census <note>original: "numbring"</note> of the people which *David* ordered was intended to show him how many fighting men he had, and consequently the strength of his kingdom's forces. Therefore, women were not counted, nor were those under twenty years old. But since *David's* action was unlawful, it received a punishment corresponding to the offense. *David* intended to glorify himself through the number of his soldiers, so God reduced the number of his soldiers by sending a deadly plague <note>original: "mortality"</note> among them. It did not reach anyone whose sex or age made them incapable of war and whom *David* had not counted; instead, it struck only those who had been the source of his pride, *2 Samuel 24:9*.

### A necessary consideration of the words of Psalm 26:6.

*I washed my hands in innocence, and so have I surrounded <note>original: "compassed"</note> your altar.* When the priests were about to officiate, they used to wash their hands and feet in the outer court; once that was done, they approached the altar. *David*, therefore, speaks of what the priests used to do. But *David* was of a tribe from which no one ever assisted at the altar, according to *Hebrews 7:13, 14*. How then does he say that he surrounded the altar? Certainly, he could not speak this literally of himself; it is, therefore, only an allusion to that act of the priests. Or, it is a Psalm composed <note>original: "indited"</note>—as many others were—for the priests and Levites to be pronounced on behalf of *David*; and this indeed is the most probable explanation. For we must by no means believe that *David* ever performed that priestly <term>Sacerdotal: relating to the functions or status of a priest</term> act. The consequences would

### A Reduction of the Titles of Solomon from one of his Books to the other.

In his first book, the *Proverbs*, he is styled the King of the Israelites—King of the entire nation. In his second book, called *Ecclesiastes*, he is named only King of Jerusalem—King of but one city. Indeed, he is not even called King *of* Jerusalem, but King *in* Jerusalem, a title that is even more diminished. But in the *Song of Songs* <note>original: "Canticle of Canticles"</note>, his last work, he has neither title nor descriptive name <term>Epithet: a word or phrase used to describe a person's attributes or qualities</term>, but only his own name. In the first two books he was a king, but in the last he is only Solomon.

This reminds me of a monarch of the last century who, during his final illness, would not be greeted by any title of honor, but commanded that they should only call him by his Christian name. <note>This likely refers to Emperor Charles V (1500–1558), who abdicated his many titles to live in a monastery before his death.</note>

<summary>This page observes a spiritual progression of humility in King Solomon's titles, noting how he moves from "King of Israel" in Proverbs to simply "Solomon" in the Song of Songs.</summary>
<keywords>Solomon, Proverbs, Ecclesiastes, Song of Songs, titles, humility, royalty, Jerusalem</keywords>

### Why the Spouse in the Song of Solomon speaks before the Bridegroom.

When *Eve* was presented to *Adam*, *Adam* spoke first; and according to all reason, it belongs to the bridegroom to begin the conversation and to express his affections. How much more, then, does it belong to Jesus Christ to speak first in the dialogue between him and the Church, his spouse? Why then does she precede <note>original: "prevent"; here meaning to go before or anticipate</note> him in this song? 

Certainly, the bridegroom had already spoken to the heart of his spouse before she ever opened her mouth; indeed, he dictated to her what she was to say. The first words she pronounced, which are the first of this song <note>original: "Canticle"</note>, follow those that the bridegroom had already spoken to her in her soul. She believed, therefore she spoke. If this song had only a physical <note>original: "carnal"</note> marriage as its subject, it would be against the natural order—and indeed, against good manners—for the bride to make the proposal and to court a man to have him as her husband.

<summary>This page addresses the theological question of why the Bride (representing the Church) speaks first in the Song of Solomon, concluding that her words are actually a response to Christ’s prior, internal communication to her soul.</summary>
<keywords>Song of Solomon, Bridegroom, Spouse, Jesus Christ, Church, Adam and Eve, Canticles, spiritual marriage, prayer</keywords>

### *The Angels, having eaten the bread of men, returned the favor by causing men to eat the Bread of Angels—though this bread was far more substantial than the bread they had received from human hands.*

The Angels had been entertained at *Abraham’s* and *Lot’s* tables, and later, they brought bread to the Prophet *Elijah*. That food which was given to him by an Angel had such power <note>original: "vertue"; meaning inherent strength, efficacy, or miraculous quality</note> that it enabled him to walk for forty days and forty nights without stopping (1 Kings 19). Whether *Manna*—which the Scripture calls the "Bread of Angels" in *Psalm 78*—was created or merely distributed by those celestial spirits is a matter of much debate. However, regarding *Elijah*, it is evident that an Angel brought him both his food and his drink. Some might think that the Angels were willing to repay the hospitality <note>Angels appeared as men to Abraham and Lot, who offered them meals</note> that men had shown to them; but the food they provided to men had a power far above any food men could present to the Angels.

<summary>This page explores the concept of "Bread of Angels," comparing the physical meals humans provided to angels in the Bible with the supernatural, life-sustaining food angels later provided to the Prophet Elijah.</summary>
<keywords>Angels, Bread of Angels, Abraham, Lot, Elijah, 1 Kings 19, Manna, Psalm 78, hospitality, divine nourishment</keywords>

--- Page 190 ---
<meta>This page continues the comparison between the food of men and the "Bread of Angels," specifically contrasting the Angel's provision of food to Elijah with the first recorded interaction between Angels and humanity in the Garden of Eden.</meta>

### The action of that Angel who brought food <note>original: "meat"; in this period, "meat" referred to any kind of solid food or sustenance, not just animal flesh</note> to Elijah was quite contrary to the first action that Angels ever performed toward humanity.

Their first action toward man was to close the entrance to the Garden of *Eden* against him, to prevent him from eating its fruit, and particularly the fruit of the Tree of Life. They then took away from man his excellent, natural food so that he would have to work with pain to obtain an inferior kind. However, later, in the person of *Elijah*, they brought to man a more excellent food, possessing greater power <note>original: "vertue"; meaning medicinal or miraculous efficacy</note> than all the foods of the world. This was an action completely opposite to the very first thing they ever did toward mankind.

The first point <note>referring to God punishing those who plunder the temples of idols</note> is witnessed by history: among many examples, we have that of *Antiochus*, recorded in *1 Maccabees 6* and *2 Maccabees 9*. <note>Antiochus IV Epiphanes died in agony and madness after attempting to loot the temple of Artemis in Persia; the author interprets this as divine punishment for sacrilege.</note>

As for the second point, it is well known how *Nebuchadnezzar* prospered, even after he had destroyed the Temple of God. This is very noteworthy: after *Nebuchadnezzar* had taken the city of *Tyre*, God further rewarded his efforts by granting him the kingdom of *Egypt* (*Ezekiel 29:16, etc.*). 

But how is it that God involves himself in the violation of the temples of idolaters? What does His justice have to do with the nothingness <note>original: "nullity"; the state of being non-existent or having no power</note> of false gods, that He should take up their cause? To this we may answer:

1. Whoever raises himself up against what he believes to be God acts against the guidance of his own conscience, which forbids him from acting against the Divine. For although his blinded conscience makes him mistake imaginary deities for his Creator, it is still a crime to make war against that which the conscience commands one to fear and honor as a deity.

2. Those who plundered their false gods,

The other question, concerning the prosperity of *Nebuchadnezzar*, seems more difficult to resolve; for the Scripture attests that God gave success to him as a reward. But must a public thief—the scourge and plague <note>original: "Pest"</note> of mankind—be rewarded with such a payout?

We should observe that God’s justice, being willing to punish the *Jews* and the nations around them, caused the decree <note>original: "Arrest"; here meaning a formal judicial decision or sentence</note> of that punishment to be announced to them, and made *Nebuchadnezzar* the executioner. These predictions are frequent in the books of *Jeremiah* <note>original: "Jeremy"</note> and *Ezekiel*, declaring to them that *Nebuchadnezzar* would subdue them; but they did not believe them. But God, who has an interest in the honor of His messengers, verified their prophecies, particularly those regarding the destruction of the Temple. He showed, according to His predictions, that *Nebuchadnezzar* was employed by Him and fought under His banner to destroy the state of the *Jews* and the Temple itself. It is then no wonder if God gave victories to him, since he executed the decrees of Heaven—even if he did so for his own advantage, like a mercenary servant.

### Why Daniel, having foreseen the calamity of Nebuchadnezzar, advised him to repent; and having foreseen the ruin of Belshazzar, said nothing to him of repentance.

These two accounts are found in the fourth and fifth chapters of *Daniel*. He interpreted to *Nebuchadnezzar* his dream, which foretold <note>original: "denounced"</note> for him a madness of seven years and that he should be driven out of his kingdom. In order that this monarch might prevent this misfortune, *Daniel* exhorted him to forsake his sins and to do works of justice <note>In this context, "justice" refers to righteousness or acts of charity</note>.

A long time afterward, *Belshazzar*—the son and successor of *Nebuchadnezzar*—was also condemned to lose his kingdom. *Daniel* read and interpreted the sentence to him, but did not give him the advice he had given to *Nebuchadnezzar*; he did not speak to him of amending his life to avoid his ruin.

We must know that God has set a specific time for the repentance of sinners, as we have already seen. To some He gives...

--- Page 194 ---
<meta>This page concludes the comparison between Nebuchadnezzar and Belshazzar, explaining that God grants varying periods for repentance, and once that time expires, judgment becomes inevitable.</meta>

<page-num>158</page-num>

...gives a longer time, and to some a shorter time, within which they are to repent. If they allow the time that God has set <note>original: "prefixed"</note> for them to expire, their repentance comes too late to prevent their disaster; and it happens to them as it did to *Esau*, who did not arrive soon enough to receive the blessing.

However, the prophetic spirit within *Daniel* showed him that the repentance of *Nebuchadnezzar* might still come in time <note>original: "seasonably"</note> to avert the storm that threatened him. But this was not the case for *Belshazzar*; the window of time in which he was supposed to repent had already passed. Therefore, the Prophet, being divinely instructed, did not speak to him about ways to prevent a ruin that was now unavoidable.

## GENERAL TABLES.

### The Years of Years.

#### Each of these consists of 365 solar years <note>original: "Solary years"</note>, which are as many years as there are days in a year.

The first "year of years" ends, then, in the 365th year of the world, of the sun, and of *Adam*.

The second "year of years" ends at the

The year of the world 730 was exactly 200 years before the death of *Adam*, for he died in the year 930. Thus, the first man lived two "Ages" <note>The author uses "Age" here to refer to his "year of years" cycle of 365 years.</note> beyond the second "year of years."

The third, fourth, and fifth "year of years" ended within the life of *Noah*. That Patriarch saw the end of three "years of years": two before the Flood, and one after.

The sixth "year of years" ended in the year 2190, which was seven years after the death of *Abraham*.

The seventh "year of years" ended in the year 2555. At that time, the Sun stood still <note>original: "Sun stand still"; a reference to the Book of Joshua 10:13, where the sun stopped in the sky during a battle.</note>; as he was ending his seventh "year of years," he observed a Sabbath for the duration of a whole day.

The eighth and ninth "years of years" ended in the year 3285; and the year following, which was the year 3286, began the tenth "year of years." Now, in the tenth year of this tenth "year of years"—in the year 3295—the Sun went back ten degrees <note>original: "Sun went back"; a reference to 2 Kings 20:11, regarding the miraculous sign given to King Hezekiah.</note>. Thus, his going back of ten degrees took place in the tenth year of his tenth "year of years." In this way, the Sun essentially paid a "Tenth" or Tithe <note>A tithe is a tenth part of something given as an offering to God.</note> to his Creator.

### *The Prophetical years, each of which consists of 360 solar years.*

The first and second Prophetical years ended in the year of the world <note>original: "year of the world," or Anno Mundi (AM), a calendar counting from the biblical creation.</note> 720. At that time, *Enoch*, the seventh man from *Adam* (and who prophesied), was 14 "weeks of years" <note>A "week of years" is a period of seven years; 14 weeks of years equals 98 years.</note> old. His son *Methuselah*, who lived the longest of all men, was then 33 years of age—matching the number of years Christ lived upon the earth.

The third, fourth, fifth, and sixth Prophetical years ended in the year 2160; this was 77 years after the Promise <note>The covenant or promise made by God to Abraham (Genesis 12).</note>.

The seventh Prophetical year ended in the year 2520; it was seven years after the Exodus <note>original: "coming out of Egypt."</note> from *Egypt*.

The eighth, ninth, and tenth Prophetical years ended in the year 3600, which also marked the conclusion of the writing of the Old Testament. From that point until the death of Christ in the year 3960, exactly one more Prophetical year passed, which was the eleventh.

### The Thirty-three Periods of 120 Years, Ending in the Thirty-third and Final Year of Christ’s Life on Earth.

The three most remarkable periods are as follows:

The twenty-fifth period, which ended in the same year that the construction of the First Temple <note>The temple built by King Solomon in Jerusalem.</note> was finished—specifically <note>original: "viz." (videlicet), meaning "namely" or "that is to say."</note> in the year 3000. This is because twenty-five periods of 120 years had passed since the Creation.

The thirtieth period, which ended alongside the tenth Prophetical year in the year 3600. Since the number of these periods corresponds to the number of years in Christ’s life, we should note a significant difference here. At the end of this thirtieth period of 120 years, the history of the Old Testament concluded, prophetic inspirations ceased, and the Holy Spirit <note>original: "holy Ghost."</note> stopped appearing in an extraordinary way. However, at the beginning of the thirtieth year of Christ’s life, the Holy Spirit descended upon him with majesty, and the voice of God—which had not been heard for a very long time—made itself clearly understood. <note>This refers to the baptism of Jesus, where the Holy Spirit descended as a dove and God’s voice spoke from heaven.</note>

The thirty-third period of 120 years was completed during the thirty-third year of Christ’s life. <note>According to the author's calculation, 33 periods of 120 years equals 3,960 years, which aligns with his date for the death of Christ.</note>

# The Seven-Year Cycles <note>original: "Septenaries"</note> of Years from the Creation of the First Adam to the Conception of the Second <note>The "Second Adam" is a traditional theological title for Jesus Christ.</note>.

This list <note>original: "Catalogue"</note> begins with the first seven years of the world. After that, there are seven times seventy years <note>490 years</note>, which brings us to the year 497. Following the year 497, there are seven times seventy "weeks of years" <note>A "week of years" is a seven-year period, a concept from the Book of Daniel; 70 weeks of years equals 490 years.</note>. Or, to put it another way, there are seven groups of seventy weeks of years <note>70 weeks of years (490) multiplied by 7 equals 3,430 years</note> which follow the year of the world 497.

*Of these seven groups of seventy, here are the most remarkable at their conclusion:*

The first ends at the translation <note>The event where Enoch was taken into heaven by God without dying.</note> of *Enoch* <note>original: "Enoc"</note>, the seventh man after *Adam*, in the year 987.

The sixth ends at the Captivity of *Babylon* in the year 3437. After that year, to fulfill the seventy years of the Captivity, thirty-three more years passed—exactly as many years as *Christ* afterward lived upon the earth.

# THE SPECIFIC TABLES OF The Years of the World.

->*The Seventh Year*<-

This year is not notable for any specific event, yet it is distinguished; it is the first of all the Sabbatical years <note>Sabbatical years: Based on the biblical law in Leviticus 25, every seventh year the land was to lie fallow and debts were to be canceled.</note>. This seven-year period <term>Septenary</term> has since been made famous in many significant events. I will not dwell on examples which other writers have already observed; instead, I will present some instances where the observation is unique to our own study. These include the seven final years of *Methuselah*, the seventh year after the death of *Abraham*, the seventh year after the departure from *Egypt*, the seventh year of the paralysis <note>original: "Palsey"</note> of the man who was cured by the Son of God (*John 5:5*), and others which we shall mention in their appropriate places.

->*The year 1757.*<-

The birth <note>original: "nativity"</note> of Peleg <note>original: "Phaleg"</note> was followed by the division of languages <note>The "division of tongues" refers to the Tower of Babel event</note>. From this point until the death of Eber <note>original: "Heber"</note>, the father of the Hebrews, in the year 2187, there were 430 years. There was the same amount of time from the Promise <note>The promise given by God to Abraham</note> in the year 2082 <note>The OCR reads 3082, but the calculation of 430 years requires 2082</note> to the giving of the Law in the year 2513. Likewise, there was another 430 years from the Law to the anointing of David in Bethlehem in the year 2943.

The division of languages was followed by the establishment of the first and most ancient monarchy in the person of **Nimrod**, who was descended from Ham <note>original: "Cham"</note>. That cursed race <note>A reference to the biblical "Curse of Ham" used in early modern times to explain the origins of different nations</note> had the honor of founding the first empire.

***

->*The year 2008.*<-

The birth of **Abraham** occurred two years after the death of **Noah**. He lived 175 years, which is exactly half of the 350 years that Noah lived after the Flood.

***

->*The year 2094.*<-

The second time the angels showed themselves to men (*Genesis 16:7, etc.*).

This was the time of the Captivity.

->***The year 2570.***<-

**Joshua** dies, having lived 110 years. His age was forty years more than the seventy years that was the ordinary lifespan of the **Israelites** who spent forty years in the wilderness (*Psalm 90:10*).

He was then fifty-five years of age and lived fifty-five more, which amounts to 110 in total. ->*The year 2989.*<-

Some attribute to that year (which is the second to last year of the reign of *David*) the pestilence <note>original: "Pestilence"; a devastating plague or epidemic, in this case the one sent by God after David's census as described in 2 Samuel 24.</note> that God sent. --- Page 224 ---
<meta>This page continues the chronological history of the Kings of Israel and Judah, moving from the death of David to the start of Solomon's reign and explaining the theological purpose behind the brevity of their rules.</meta>

How did it happen that those kings, whom God had established, had such short lives? In order that the people of God might look forward to the Kingdom of the Messiah <note>original: "Messias"</note>, it was necessary that the earthly <note>original: "temporal"</note> Kingdom of Judah should be of short duration. Within that short span, God intended for a great diversity of both kings and events to be seen, for several <note>original: "divers"</note> reasons. That diversity would not have been as great or as abundant if there had been only a small number of kings; and their number would have been very few if each of them had lived a long time, as just three or four kings would have filled the entire duration of that kingdom. It was therefore necessary <note>original: "expedient"</note> that one should soon give way to another. This is the reason why their lives were so short compared to many other men who were their contemporaries, such as Barzillai and Jehoiada <note>original: "Jehoiadah"; see 2 Samuel 19 and 2 Chronicles 24.</note>.

In that same year, 2990, Solomon began his reign. He and his descendants <note>original: "race"</note> held the Kingdom of Judah until the year 3420, ending with Zedekiah <note>original: "Zedekia"</note>. This period lasted 430 years—a span of time equal to the period from the Promise to the Law <note>This refers to the traditional 430-year period between God's promise to Abraham and the giving of the Law at Sinai, as mentioned in Exodus 12:40 and Galatians 3:17.</note>.

<vocab>Messiah, Judah, Barzillai, Jehoiada, Solomon, Zedekiah, Kingdom of Judah</vocab>

<summary>This page explains the theological reasoning for the short lifespans of the Kings of Judah—intended to focus the people's hope on the Messiah—and provides a 430-year timeline for the Davidic dynasty.</summary>
<keywords>Solomon, Kingdom of Judah, Messiah, biblical chronology, Zedekiah, Davidic line, Barzillai, Jehoiada</keywords>

->*The year 3000*<-

This marks the end of the twenty-fifth period of 120 years.

The Temple was finished. They had spent seven years <note>original: "one week of years"</note> building it; and from its completion, it remained for 420 years <note>original: "sixty weeks of years"</note>, until the year 3420.

That same total of 420 years <note>original: "sixty weeks of years"</note> is equal to fourteen periods of thirty years. After the Temple was finished, it stood for fourteen periods of thirty years. During the first thirty years, it was acknowledged and visited by all the tribes of *Israel*. However, at the end of those thirty years, ten tribes abandoned the Temple to worship in *Dan* and *Bethel* <note>Following the death of Solomon, the kingdom split; Jeroboam established rival worship centers in these cities to prevent his subjects from traveling to Jerusalem. See 1 Kings 12.</note>. The thirteen thirty-year periods that followed the first correspond to the number of the thirteen tribes, including the tribe of *Levi*, whom the rebellious <note>original: "apostate"</note> *Israelites* had cast aside <note>The Levites were removed from their priestly duties in the northern kingdom by Jeroboam, leading many to migrate to Judah.</note>.

->*The year 3030.*<-

The Temple had stood for thirty years when the ten tribes abandoned it. Christ, the true Temple, was thirty years old when he said to the *Jews*, "*Destroy this temple*" <note>See John 2:19-21.</note>.

# READER,

THE Jews have many *principles*, which at first sight seem to be indifferent <note>original: "indifferent"; meaning neutral or of no consequence</note>, but indeed they do nullify the authority of the *New Testament*. He who lets them pass without contradiction unconsciously renounces *Christianity*. They are the inventions of their *Rabbis*, whose traditions pass for oracles <note>meaning: divine revelations or authoritative voices</note> among them. You shall cite either *Moses* or the *Prophets* to them only in vain, as long as the *Jew* grants the *Rabbis* a status collateral <note>meaning: parallel or equal</note> to them, with an equal right of suffrage <note>original: "suffrage"; here meaning an authoritative voice or the right to provide a deciding opinion</note>. Those men are greatly deceived who, in disputing with such *adversaries*, believe—

<summary>In this preface, the author warns that certain Jewish rabbinical traditions undermine the authority of the New Testament and argues that citing the Old Testament is ineffective if the Rabbis are given equal authority to the Prophets.</summary>

<keywords>Judaism, Rabbis, New Testament, Christianity, Moses, Prophets, religious polemic, authority of scripture</keywords>

--- Page 244 ---
<meta>This page continues the sentence from the previous page: "Those men are greatly deceived who, in disputing with such adversaries, believe..."</meta>

believe, that they must at first come directly to the *Question* concerning the *Messiah*. On the contrary, we ought to begin by overturning their *Principles*, namely, *Their Traditions*. These traditions are destroyed if we show them that they consist of obvious *blasphemies*, gross *absurdities*, mythical *tales*, formal *contradictions*, and other *monstrosities*, which are repugnant even to common sense. I have shown some small samples <note>original: "scantlings"; meaning small portions, glimpses, or samples</note> of this in a short verbal *conference* I had lately with one of the most famous *Rabbis* of this age; had he accepted the continuation of it—either by word of mouth or by writing, as I offered him—I am certain he would have had a thousand times worse success.

### The First MAXIM of the *Jews*.

One angel *is never employed in two missions <note>original: "Embassies"</note>, or in carrying two messages.* **Genesis Rabbah** <note>original: *Bereshith-Rabba*; a collection of ancient rabbinic interpretations of the Book of Genesis</note>, quoted by **Rabbi Moses the Egyptian** <note>a reference to the 12th-century philosopher Maimonides</note>; otherwise known as **Maimonides** <note>original: "Bar-Majemon"</note>.

### The Examination.

If that is granted to them, we must renounce the entire New Testament, which they attempt to destroy at its root. We read there that the Angel *Gabriel*, having delivered a message to *Zechariah* <note>original: "Zachary"</note>, the father of *John the Baptist*, carried another message six months later to the *Virgin Mary*. This could not be the case if this Jewish maxim were true. Moreover, the same Angel *Gabriel* had performed the office of a messenger a long time before,

messenger, being sent to *Daniel*. From this, it would follow that after that time he never carried any message, and, consequently, never spoke either to *Zechariah* <note>original: "Zachary"</note> or the *Virgin Mary*.

But these blind Jews <note>The author uses "blind" here in a polemical sense, common in 17th-century religious debates, to mean "spiritually unseeing."</note> have not noticed that; for even the same Angel *Gabriel*, in the Old Testament, carried two different <note>original: "several"</note> messages at different times to the same person—namely, to *Daniel* (chapters 8 and 9). The first was in the third year of *Belshazzar*'s reign, and the other in the first year of *Darius*. One of them concerned the four empires, and the other concerned the death of Christ, the destruction of the Second Temple, and the determination <note>original: "prefixion"</note> of the seventy weeks.

Moreover, what problem would arise if one angel were employed in two messages? On the contrary, it was appropriate that the same angel who had already spoken to men should be sent to them again for greater confirmation. Thus, when *Daniel* was sorrowful, God sent him a message by the same angel who had spoken to him previously (*Daniel* 9:21). And as for *Zechariah* and the *Virgin*, God sent them word by the same angel who had marked the seventy weeks; from this, they might conclude that the time of the Redemption was drawing near. On the contrary, it was appropriate, and it has contributed <note>original: "conduced"</note> to the strengthening of the faith of those who were waiting for Redemption, that the same messenger who had foretold the time should return toward the end of that period. His purpose was to remind them of his prophecy and to inform <note>original: "advertise"</note> them that its fulfillment was at hand. In this especially, we may see the effect of the wisdom of God.

Moreover, we might cite <note>original: "alleage"</note> to the *Jews* the example of that angel who appeared twice to the wife of *Manoah* (*Judges* 13:13, etc.). These were two distinct <note>original: "several"</note> journeys and two distinct messages, although both aimed at the same goal and represented the same subject.

In short, this maxim of the *Jews* even contradicts the Old Testament.

### The Second Maxim of the *Jews*.

**T**wo angels are never employed in carrying a single message. This is found in the place mentioned above, namely <note>original: "viz." from the Latin *videlicet*</note> in *Bereshith-Rabba* <note>A Midrashic collection of ancient Jewish interpretations of the Book of Genesis.</note>.

### The Examination.

This assertion by the *Jews* is different from the previous one, but it aims toward the same goal: to contradict <note>original: "give the lie to"</note> the *New Testament* regarding two fundamental articles of faith, namely the Resurrection and the Ascension of our Lord. For we read that his Resurrection was declared by two angels acting together; and likewise, as he ascended into heaven, two angels together at the same time and place spoke to the Apostles (*Luke* 24:4, etc.; *John* 20:12, etc.; *Acts* 1:10, etc.). Now, those historical accounts would be false if it were true that two angels were never sent together with one message.

But here again we may observe the blindness of the *Jews* in the midst of the

Can they deny that two—or even three—angels were sent together to speak to *Abraham*? And although one of them was the leader <note>original: "chiefest"</note>, did they not all speak to him? (*Genesis* 18:9). And even if only one of them was meant to deliver the specific message, did all three not come for the same purpose regarding the birth of *Isaac*? What then will become of this maxim of the *Jews*, which contradicts such a famous example from the Old Testament?

They should also have considered something very remarkable regarding this issue. When God has employed two or many angels to deliver the same message, His purpose <note>original: "end"</note> was that they should not only be messengers, but also witnesses to the truth of His words. For just as among people, we rely on the testimony <note>original: "depositions"</note> of two or three witnesses, so the testimony of two or three angels has confirmed the faith of those to whom they have spoken. We have already observed this in the *Harmony of the Times* <note>Likely a reference to a chronological or theological work by the same author or a contemporary.</note>.

### The Third Maxim of the Jews.

No man can become a *Prophet unless he is born a Hebrew.* — Rabbi Menasseh Ben-Israel, *The Conciliator on the Pentateuch* <note>original: "Conciliat. in Pentateuch." Menasseh Ben-Israel (1604–1657) was a famous Portuguese rabbi and author who sought to reconcile seemingly contradictory passages in the Five Books of Moses.</note>.

### The Examination.

For a long time, their ancestors have spoken in this same manner, saying that the *Holy Spirit*—that is, His extraordinary gifts—*never descends upon a Gentile* <note>A person who is not Jewish.</note>. However, this prejudice of theirs has been proven false. For, to their amazement, they have seen that the *Gentiles* have received the miraculous gifts of the Holy Spirit, even the gift of speaking in different languages <note>original: "Tongues"</note>. This was a gift which no Prophet ever possessed under the Old Testament (*Acts* 10:44 <note>The original text cites Acts 16:44, but the account of the Holy Spirit falling on Gentiles occurs in Acts 10:44.</note>, etc.). 

Furthermore, God Himself had forewarned that He would pour out His Spirit upon *all people* <note>original: "all flesh"</note> (*Joel* 2:28). Thus, He was far from promising to forever confine His Spirit to their nation, or to make it hereditary to them alone, while excluding <note>original: "debar"</note> all other nations from it.

Their Rabbis themselves are not all of the same opinion about this point. One of the most famous among them, Rabbi *David Kimchi* <note>David Kimchi (1160–1235), also known as Radak, was a preeminent medieval Jewish biblical commentator and grammarian whose work remains highly influential.</note>, holds that the Prophet *Obadiah* was not of the lineage of *Israel*, but was a convert <note>original: "Proselyte"</note> from *Idumea* <note>Idumea is the Greek name for Edom, a region south of Judea inhabited by the descendants of Esau.</note>. If the *Jews* reply that the *Idumeans* were *Hebrews* because they descended from *Abraham*, it would then follow that the spirit of prophecy was not unique <note>original: "peculiar"</note> to *Israel*, but could be shared with other nations. But by the word *Hebrews*, they mean only the *Israelites*, so that they might try—though in vain—to claim the gift of prophecy exclusively for themselves.

They should have considered instead that *Judaism* has had no prophets since they rejected Christ; for has any among them possessed the spirit of prophecy since then? On the contrary, *Christianity* and its beginnings have been animated by that Spirit which has authorized it—not only by the gift of prophecy, but also by the gift of healing, the working of miracles <note>original: "wonders"</note>, mighty works, the discerning of spirits, the variety of languages <note>original: "Tongues"</note>, and the interpretation of those languages (*1 Corinthians 12:9, 10*). How does it happen that the spirit of prophecy has not assisted *Judaism* against the *Christians*, but instead has supplied the

*Christians* against *Judaism*? Or if they deny that the spirit of prophecy <note>original: "Prophesie"</note> has been with the first *Christians*, how does it happen that that spirit never came down upon any *Jew* to oppose *Christianity*? In the past, *Moses* opposed *Pharaoh's* magicians, and *Elijah* <note>original: "Elija"</note> opposed the false prophets of *Ahab*. Likewise, *Micaiah* <note>original: "Micaiju"; the prophet who stood against the 400 false prophets in 1 Kings 22.</note> opposed *Zedekiah* <note>The false prophet Zedekiah son of Chenaanah.</note>, and *Jeremiah* <note>original: "Jeremia"</note> opposed many impostors who bragged much of revelations that contradicted his own. If ever the *Jews* had need of the spirit of prophecy, it was then, when *Christianity* began to appear and the destruction of the Second Temple was drawing near; but that spirit was gone from them, and they never possessed it afterward.

Several reasons have led us to provide a commentary on this principle <note>original: "Maxim"</note> of the Rabbis. 
1. The *Jews* build other arguments upon this one, which will follow in order. 
2. They intend to contradict the New Testament, where *David* and *Daniel* are explicitly called prophets (*Acts 2:30–31*, *Matthew 24:15*). 
3. Their boldness is remarkable, to take away the status of a prophet from those whom they admit to have spoken by an extraordinary gift of the Holy Spirit, and to have received in-

structions from the mouths of angels, and through dreams and divine visions. 4. They speak in contradictory terms. 5. They show how weak their reasoning is.

For first, concerning *David*: What makes them say that God never spoke immediately to him? *David* tells us the contrary in the same text (*2 Samuel 23:2*): *The Spirit of the Lord spoke by me.* 2. But they say, *"Even so, after the dream and its interpretation, he still calls it a dream"* (*Daniel 2:19 and 7:1, 2, 15, and 8:27*). What of that? Because, they say, he repeats that they happened to him in a dream. What a wild argument this is to degrade a prophet! Is there any spark of judgment in this discourse?

Some will ask, do they not acknowledge that the *Psalms* and the Book of *Daniel* proceeded from the Holy Spirit, and are canonical and true parts of the Old Testament? They do indeed confess it and give them the title of *Hagiographa* <note>original: "Hagiographes"; from the Greek for "holy writings," referring to the *Ketuvim*, the third section of the Hebrew Bible which includes Psalms and Daniel.</note>. Why then do they not give them the title of *Prophetical*? It is a *Jewish logomachy* <note>A dispute about words rather than reality.</note>, or a frivolous distinction. But it was necessary for us to note this, because the *Jews* would make use of it against the arguments we raise against some of their other maxims, as we shall see in the proper place.

### The Fifth MAXIM of the *Jews*.

Prophecy withdraws from a man who is a captive, or in slavery, or in banishment, and is not master of himself. <note>Cited from the Proverbs of Ben Sira and Rabbi Moses (Maimonides).</note>

## The Examination.

If we should grant them this, then the entire Book of Revelation <note>original: "Apocalypse"</note>, which concludes <note>original: "shutteth up"</note> the New Testament—and indeed all such prophecies—would be declared void. For it is said that Saint John received it while in exile, confined to a very small island <note>Patmos</note> where he was not his own master but lived as a prisoner.

But who told them that the Spirit of Prophecy is never found in a captive, or in someone who does not have control <note>original: "disposal"</note> over their own person? Do they fear that the Spirit itself would be a prisoner just because its host was a prisoner? Was it not in a prison that Joseph foretold what would happen to the two officers of Pharaoh, even naming the very day? If that prediction was not prophetic, what then should we call it? In fact, it was in prison where the Spirit of Prophecy first began to descend upon Joseph, who had never experienced it before.

### The Sixth Maxim of the *Jews*.

In times of persecution, prophecy ceases; especially when we are under the yoke of secular <note>original: "Profane"</note> nations. — Rabbi Moses <note>This refers to Maimonides, a preeminent medieval Jewish philosopher also known as Rambam</note>.

### The Examination.

This Maxim is included in the previous one and is refuted by the same reasons.

<summary>The author concludes that physical confinement and political oppression do not stop prophecy, citing the Book of Revelation and the experiences of the Apostles as evidence against the Rabbinical claim that prophecy ceases during persecution.</summary>
<keywords>prophecy, captivity, Apocalypse, John the Apostle, Paul and Silas, Rabbi Moses, Maimonides, persecution, Acts of the Apostles</keywords>

--- Page 259 ---
<meta>This page continues the author's critique of the Jewish claim that prophecy is impossible during times of persecution or captivity, following the discussion of the Apostles and the Book of Revelation.</meta>

reasons; but we will add to that what follows.

The intention of the *Jews* is to anticipate the objection we make against them—namely, that for so long now they have had no prophets. In contrast, during their bondage in *Egypt* and their captivity in *Babylon*, God never left them without prophets, except for a very short time. To this they answer that a man who is persecuted, or a subject to other nations, is incapable of receiving the Spirit of Prophecy. They claim that because they are all currently in this dispersion <note>the Diaspora, or the scattering of the Jewish people</note> and are persecuted, none of them can possess the Spirit of Prophecy. They argue that even if there were any prophets among them, the gift of prophecy would leave them as soon as they began to be persecuted.

According to this view, it is within the power of persecutors to hinder the Spirit of God, to stop His influence, and to prevent Him from assisting those persecuted for His sake through His revelations—even when they stand in greatest need of His presence.

But that impiety is self-refuting and is condemned by the examples we have already cited, especially that of *Jeremiah*, who was persecuted even to the point of imprisonment and yet still received new prophecies. To which we add this: When did *Elijah* hear the voice of God in *Horeb* <note>Mount Horeb, where Elijah fled to escape the persecution of Queen Jezebel</note>?

<summary>The author refutes the Jewish argument that prophecy ceases during persecution, arguing that such a view wrongly implies that human oppressors can block God's Spirit, and cites the prophet Jeremiah as a counter-example.</summary>
<keywords>prophecy, persecution, Jewish-Christian polemics, Jeremiah, Elijah, Horeb, Spirit of God, Diaspora, captivity</keywords>

--- Page 260 ---
<meta>This page completes the sentence from the previous page, continuing the argument that persecution does not prevent prophecy by citing historical examples of biblical prophets who received revelations while suffering or in danger.</meta>

Mount *Horeb*, and received from Him the prophecy concerning *Hazael*, *Elisha*, and *Jehu*? It was when *Elijah* was fleeing from *Jezebel*, who was hunting him to put him to death (*1 Kings 19*). When did *Micaiah* <note>original: "Micajah"</note> predict the disastrous outcome of *Ahab’s* battle? It was when *Micaiah*, after being struck on the cheek, was condemned to prison to be treated harshly <note>original: "hardly used"</note> (*1 Kings 22:27*). 

When did *Amos* prophesy to *Amaziah* the priest that his wife would become a prostitute in the city, that his sons and daughters would die in war <note>original: "fall by the sword"</note>, that his land would be measured and divided, that he would die in a pagan <note>original: "polluted"</note> land, and that the people of *Israel* would certainly be taken into captivity? All these things were revealed to the prophet *Amos* when he was accused of high treason and was about to be banished from the land of *Israel* (*Amos 7:10*). 

Therefore, it is not true that persecution can stop prophecy. The true cause and the time when prophecies and visions would cease are recorded in *Daniel*, chapter 9, verse 24. If Jewish readers look at that passage, they should consider it carefully.

### The Seventh Maxim of the Jews.

The Holy Spirit <note>original: "holy Ghost"</note> does not rest upon a man who is sad, but on him who is merry and cheerful. (In the *Ethics of the Fathers* <note>original: *Pirke Aboth*</note>, the *Proverbs of Ben Sira*, and Rabbi Moses [Maimonides] in *The Guide for the Perplexed* <note>original: *Doct. Perplex.*, referring to the Latin title *Doctor Perplexorum*</note>).

### The Examination.

That maxim is very lighthearted <note>original: "jolly"</note>, but it is refuted by the Sacred History. Even if they only understand the Holy Spirit to mean the gift of prophecy, it is still not true that it was never conferred on anyone except those who were in a state of mirth. What then? If a man is afflicted for his sins, is he incapable of the spirit of prophecy while he remains in such a justifiable sorrow—one so necessary and so pleasing to the Spirit of God? From this, a strange <note>original: "fine"</note> conclusion would follow: that there was one day every year—namely, the Day of Atonement <note>original: "Expiations"</note>—on which the prophetic spirit never descended upon men. For it was a day of mourning, when all the Israelites were

The **Jews** should instead consider that the Son of God <note>referring to Jesus Christ</note>, while weeping out of sorrow, foretold what would happen to them forty years later: that the enemies of **Jerusalem** would build a siege wall <note>original: "cast a trench"</note> around her, and that not one stone would remain upon another in that unhappy city. They themselves report that the **Romans** forced a plow to pass over the site, and that no mark of a city remained. They ought to say, on the contrary, that the most excellent prophecies occurred and were composed <note>original: "indited"</note> in sorrow—a fact we could prove with many other examples.

Old people are not capable of the Spirit of Prophecy. Almost all the Prophets were young, and only **Moses** and **Aaron** prophesied in their old age, which was miraculous.

This maxim follows the previous one and is found in the same passages we have already cited. Based on the hypothesis we have already debunked <note>original: "overthrown"</note>, they build this assertion: that an old man—because of the irritability <note>original: "peevishness"</note> caused by age and other natural ailments <note>original: "indispositions"</note>—cannot possess the gift <note>original: "faculty"</note> of prophecy, since <note>original: "sith"</note> it supposedly requires a joyful and cheerful person.

### The Ninth MAXIM of the Jews.

The Law of Moses <note>original: "Moses his Law"</note> seems to be defective in many things that are necessary. (Rabbi Manasseh Ben Israel, *The Conciliator* <note>original: "Conciliat. in Pentateuch," a work reconciling seemingly contradictory biblical passages</note> on the Pentateuch <term>the first five books of the Bible</term>.)

### The Examination.

This good Rabbi <note>original: "Rabbine"</note> does not dare to say openly that the Law is imperfect, but only that it *seems* to be so. However, if it falsely appears that way to him, he should blame his own imperfection rather than attributing it to the Law of God. His goal (along with that of his colleagues) is to authorize the opinions and the traditions of the Rabbis as necessary supplements to what they claim is missing from the Law of Moses.

What are these missing parts? These are the primary ones they claim: when the Law speaks of the months of the year, it does not mention whether they are solar or lunar <note>original: "Solary or Lunary"</note>. This is a necessary distinction so that the true time for the celebration of the festivals <note>original: "Feasts"</note> may be

be known (Exodus 12:2). It does not mention which parts of the beast have prohibited fat (Leviticus 3:17). It does not express the signs by which they might identify unclean birds (Leviticus 11:13). It does not specify which works are lawful or unlawful on the Sabbath day (Exodus 20). It does not mention whether a father may be the heir of his son (Numbers 27). nevertheless, it has preserved the rights of parents; for indirectly it calls them to the same rights it has given to uncles. As for the other point, is it not completely evident that the same prohibition which forbids a son to marry his own mother also forbids him from marrying his grandmother? And if it were necessary for the Law to explicitly name the grandmother, would it not have been equally necessary to name the great-grandmother, and all of a man’s ancestors back to infinity? We would not have known that the marriage of a man with his grandmother was unlawful unless the Rabbis <note>original: "Rabbins"</note> had told us so. They claim to have taught us something that neither the Law of Nature nor the Law of *Moses* ever taught. Where is the shame of those men!

There are angels who are corruptible, although their species <note>original: "kinds"</note> are permanent. (Rabbi Moses in *The Guide for the Perplexed* <note>original: "Doct. Perplex." referring to the Latin title *Doctor Perplexorum* for Maimonides' *Moreh Nevukhim*</note>).

### The Examination.

Here is paganism in the doctrine of the *Jews*. The heathens believe that the demons or spirits, having lived a long time, did at the last die one after another, and that yet their species <note>original: "kind"</note> was preserved in other individuals succeeding the former, as among men and other living creatures. ### The 15th MAXIM of the *Jews*.

**Cursed be those who calculate** <note>original: "reckon"</note> **the days of the** *Messiah* <note>original: "Messias"</note>.

### The Examination.

They would say that they ought not to compute the time to know when the *Messiah* shall come. The meaning is that, having expected him for so long and having measured all the ages and almost every moment to find the day of his coming—and being so often deceived in their calculation—they are resolved to calculate no longer. And this is the sense of that *Maxim* which is so common among them. Now, it cannot stand <note>original: "subsist"</note> except upon a false *Hypothesis*, namely <note>original: "viz."</note>, that God has not revealed the time when *Christ* shall come.

This deserves to be examined. We know that God has revealed the times of various <note>original: "diverse"</note> other occurrences that have happened <note>The text ends here; "happened" is provided by the catchword "hapned" from the bottom of the original page.</note>

<summary>This page addresses the 15th Jewish Maxim, which forbids calculating the date of the Messiah's arrival. The author argues that this prohibition exists only because past Jewish calculations have failed, and he disputes the underlying assumption that God has kept the timing of Christ's coming a secret.</summary>

## The Examination.

Because the Jews are as poor at chronology as they are at recording histories as if they were fables, they eclipse <note>In this context, "eclipse" means to omit or obscure.</note> 168 of those years which have passed since the Creation. The minimum age that knowledgeable men attribute to the world, at this present year, is 5,585 years; and their calculations (which we do not need to repeat here) are easily verified. However, the Rabbi makes the world 168 years younger than it actually is.

Whether this is done through ignorance or through malice, it is always important for us to observe that their calculation breaks a vast number of connections <note>original: "correspondencies"</note> between the New Testament and the Old. In fact, it contradicts <note>original: "gives the lie to"</note> both and severs the continuity of the entire history, which we are able to prove at the appropriate time and place.

### *The specific opinion of a modern author regarding the salvation of the Jews.*

---
